Request for Tender – Terms and Conditions	
RFT details
Overview
Introduction
The Commonwealth of Australia represented by the Australian Electoral Commission (AEC) invites Tenders to respond to this Request for Tender (RFT) for the Services.
Detailed information on the AEC's requirements for the Services is contained at Schedule 1 to this RFT (Statement of Requirements).  
Tenderers should refer to clause 2.3 when reading this RFT for guidance on defined terms and how clauses are to be interpreted. 
Tenders should be in the form set out in Schedule 3, including all of its Annexures.
Tenders should be submitted in accordance with clause 5 of this RFT. The AEC will exclude from the evaluation process a Tender that does not satisfy each of the Mandatory Requirements.  
Tenders will be assessed against the Evaluation Criteria.
About the AEC
The AEC is a Commonwealth agency responsible for conducting federal Electoral Events and maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll. The AEC is also responsible for undertaking electoral research, promoting community awareness of electoral and Parliamentary matters, and assisting in the conduct of approved international elections and industrial election events.
The AEC's website is at http://www.aec.gov.au.  AEC offices are organised geographically, with a National Office in Canberra, seven state and territory offices and Divisional Offices in or near each of the electoral Divisions.
Definitions and interpretation
Unless the context dictates otherwise:
terms defined in the Deed of Standing Offer or the Statement of Requirements have the same meaning in this RFT; and
the interpretation clause of the Deed of Standing Offer applies to the interpretation of this RFT.
All other capitalised terms are defined as follows for the purposes of this RFT. To the extent of any inconsistency, terms defined below will prevail over those in the Deed of Standing Offer or Statement of Requirements.
If there is any inconsistency between any provisions of this RFT the following provisions will prevail in descending order, to the extent of the inconsistency:
these Request for Tender – Terms and Conditions (clauses 1 to 10);
Schedule 2 – Draft Deed of Standing Offer;
Schedule 1 – Statement of Requirements; and
Schedule 3 – Tender Form.
Objectives and expected outcomes
The AEC expects to identify Service Providers to deliver the Services on a best value for money overall basis. 
The AEC intends to set up a panel of Service Providers, all of whom are engaged under a Deed of Standing Offer. Further information on panel arrangements is available on the Department of Finance website.
The proposed Initial Deed Period of the Deed of Standing Offer is set out in Item 7, with the Deed Option Period specified in Item 7 (if any), exercisable at the AEC’s discretion. 
Partial Tenders will only be accepted if specified in Item 8.
Indicative timetable
The proposed timetable for the release of this RFT and submission of Tenders is set out in Items 2 and 3. 
Details of any industry briefing are set out in Item 11.
The proposed timetable for the Tender evaluation process is set out in Item 9. This proposed timetable is provided for convenience only and does not identify all activities relating to this RFT process or the evaluation of Tenders. The AEC may change the proposed timetable at its absolute discretion without notification to Tenderers.
Intention to respond
Tenderers should notify the Contact Officer using the Contact Address whether they intend to response or decline to respond to this RFT within 10 Business Days of the RFT Release Date.
The notification under clause 2.6(a) is for the AEC’s information purposes only.
Failure to provide the notification under clause 2.6(a) will not preclude a Tenderer from submitting a Tender in response to this RFT.
Public Interest Certificate
A Public Interest Certificate (PIC) has not, as at the date of release of this RFT, been issued for this procurement. 
The AEC may issue a PIC at any time during the procurement process, in accordance with the requirements of the Government Procurement (Judicial Review) Act 2018 (Cth), in which case, all potential suppliers will be notified via AusTender.
Communications
AusTender, the Australian Government Tender System
AusTender is the Australian Government’s procurement information system. Access to and use of AusTender is subject to terms and conditions. In participating in this procurement process, Tenderers must comply with those terms and conditions and any applicable instructions, processes, procedures and recommendations as advised on AusTender at: https://www.tenders.gov.au/?event=public.termsOfUse.
All queries and requests for technical or operational support relating to AusTender must be directed to:
AusTender Help Desk
Telephone:  1300 651 698
International:  +61 2 6215 1558
Email:  tenders@finance.gov.au
The AusTender Help Desk is available between 9am and 5pm ACT local time, Monday to Friday (excluding ACT and national public holidays).
Communications with the AEC
All enquiries for information or notices by a Tenderer to the AEC in connection with this RFT must be: 
in writing; 
addressed to the Contact Officer; and
sent by e-mail to the Contact Address before the Enquiry Deadline.
Tenderers may seek clarification of the meaning of the content of this RFT from the Contact Officer in accordance with clause 3.2(a).  
Tenderers should immediately notify the Contact Officer in writing if they reasonably believe there is any discrepancy, error, ambiguity, inconsistency or omission in this RFT so that the AEC may take whatever corrective action, if any, it deems appropriate.  Any actual discrepancy, error, ambiguity, inconsistency, or omission in this RFT or any other Information so identified will be corrected (or the proper information issued) and will be made available to all Tenderers (without attribution to the identifying Tenderer).
Clarifications, addenda and other notices
The AEC may deliver any written notification or response to a Tenderer:
by issuing a notice using the same method as for the issue of this RFT; or 
by sending it to, or causing it to be left at, the Tenderer's address as specified in its Tender. Such notification will be deemed to be delivered in accordance with the notice clause of the Deed of Standing Offer.
In all cases the AEC reserves the right to:
forward the question and details of any AEC response to all Tenderers on a non-attributable basis; and
not respond to queries, comments or questions and does not intend to respond to enquiries received after the Enquiry Deadline.
Where the AEC issues an addendum to this RFT prior to the RFT Closing Time, it will communicate the addendum to all Tenderers via AusTender.
Responsibility of Tenderers
Tenderers who have registered and downloaded the RFT documentation will be notified by AusTender via email of the issue of any addenda, clarifications or other notices in respect of the RFT documentation. It is in the interests of Tenderers to ensure they have correctly recorded their contact details prior to downloading RFT documentation. If Tenderers have not recorded their details correctly, they should amend their details and download the RFT documentation again.
Tenderers are required to login to AusTender and collect addenda, clarifications and other notices as notified.
The AEC will accept no responsibility if a Tenderer fails to become aware of any addendum notice which would have been apparent from a visit to the AusTender page for this RFT.
If a Tenderer has obtained RFT documentation other than from AusTender, they will need to visit AusTender, register as a user and download the RFT documentation for this RFT.
Requirements of Tenders
Conditions for Participation
Subject to clauses 5.5 and 5.9(a), the AEC will exclude a Tender from further consideration if the Tenderer does not meet the following Conditions for Participation:
Minimum Content and Format Requirements
Subject to clause 5.9(a), the AEC will exclude a Tender from further consideration if the AEC considers that the Tender does not comply with any of the requirements set out below.
While not a minimum content requirement, the Tender should include all materials for consideration as annexures, attachments or otherwise within the submission documentation, no hyperlinked materials should be referenced for evaluation and may not be considered for evaluation purposes if included.
Format of Tender
Tenderers should submit a completed Tender Form. Tenderers should complete all of the Annexures to Schedule 3 and attach them to their Tender Form.  
Tenderers should ensure that their Tender contains all information requested in this RFT.  
Executive summary
Tenderers should provide a summary of their Tender in Annexure C to Schedule 3.
Tenderers should note that this executive summary may be used for high-level briefing of key stakeholders, for example, the Minister(s) and the Parliamentary Secretary, the Department of Finance, and Parliamentary Committees and should not contain any information which the Tenderer would not wish to have made available in the public domain. 
Consents for security, probity and financial checks
The AEC may perform such security, probity and financial checks and procedures as the AEC, at its absolute discretion, may determine are necessary in relation to:
a Tenderer, its officers, employees, partners, associates and subcontractors; and
Related Bodies Corporate of the Tenderer and its subcontractors and their officers, employees and subcontractors.
A Tender may be excluded from consideration by the AEC if the Tenderer does not provide, at its own cost, all reasonable assistance to the AEC in this regard. 
Contracting with a single legal entity 
The AEC intends that any Deed of Standing Offer that is awarded will be between the AEC and a single legal entity (as opposed to a group of entities or a consortium), with that single legal entity being responsible for the performance of all of the Service Provider’s obligations contained in the Deed of Standing Offer.
Where the Tenderer proposes to leverage off multiple entities to provide the Services, those entities must be subcontractors to the single legal entity who contracts with the AEC.
The AEC may exclude a Tender from the evaluation process if the Tenderer does not propose to contract with the AEC using a single legal entity that exists at the RFT Closing Time. 
Alternative Tenders
This clause 4.7 applies where Item 9 indicates that alternative Tenders will be accepted for the purposes of this RFT.
A Tenderer must submit a Tender that complies with the requirements of this RFT. However the Tenderer may also submit an alternative Tender which does not comply with the requirements of this RFT provided that a compliant Tender is also submitted. 
If a Tenderer submits an alternative Tender, they should clearly identify it as an alternative Tender and clearly state the advantages, disadvantages, limitations and capabilities of the alternative Tender.
Lodgement process
RFT Closing Time 
Tenders must be lodged before the RFT Closing Time.
Lodgement through AusTender
Tenders must be lodged electronically via AusTender before the RFT Closing Time and in accordance with the Tender lodgement procedures set out in this RFT and on AusTender.
Tenders lodged by any other means (including physically or by email) will not be accepted.
Preparing to lodge a Tender 
	Tender file formats, naming conventions and sizes
The AEC will accept Tenders lodged in Microsoft Word, Microsoft Excel and/or PDF format.
The Tender file name(s):
should incorporate the Tenderer’s legal entity name; and
should reflect the various parts of the bid they represent, where the Tender comprises multiple files.        
Tender files must not exceed a combined file size of seven megabytes per upload. 
Tenders must be completely self-contained. No hyperlinked or other material may be incorporated by reference. 
Checking for Harmful Code
In submitting their Tenders electronically, Tenderers warrant that they have taken reasonable steps to ensure that Tender files are free of Harmful Code which may affect AusTender and/or the AEC’s ICT environment. Tenders found to contain Harmful Code may be excluded from consideration.
Electronic lodgement process
Tenderers must allow sufficient time for Tender lodgement, including time that may be required for any problem analysis and resolution prior to the RFT Closing Time.
Tenderers should be aware that holding the “Lodge a Response” page in the web browser will not hold the electronic tender box open beyond the RFT Closing Time. An error message will be issued if the lodgement process is attempted after the RFT Closing Time.
Tenders lodged through AusTender will be deemed to be authorised by the Tenderer.
If Tenderers have any problem in accessing the AusTender website or uploading a Tender they should contact the AusTender Help Desk prior to the RFT Closing Time. Failure to do so may result in the Tender being excluded from consideration.
Late Tenders, incomplete Tenders and corrupted files
Any attempt to lodge a Tender after the RFT Closing Time will not be permitted by AusTender. Such a Tender will be deemed a Late Tender. Late Tenders will be excluded from consideration unless the Tender is a Late Tender as a consequence of mishandling by the AEC.
The countdown clock displayed on AusTender will display in real time the amount of time left until the RFT Closing Time. The time displayed on the countdown clock is deemed the correct amount of time left until the RFT Closing Time and will be the means by which the AEC will determine whether a Tender has been lodged by the RFT Closing Time.
Where electronic submission of a Tender has commenced prior to the RFT Closing Time but concluded after the RFT Closing Time, and upload of the Tender file/s has completed successfully, as confirmed by AusTender system logs, the Tender will not be deemed to be a Late Tender. Such Tenders will be identified by AusTender to the AEC as having commenced transmission prior to, but completed lodgement after, the RFT Closing Time.
Where a Tender lodgement consists of multiple uploads, due to the number and/or size of the files, Tenderers must ensure that transmission of all files is completed and receipted before the RFT Closing Time and clause 5.5(c) will only apply to the final upload.
Incomplete Tenders may be excluded from consideration. This includes Tenders with electronic files that cannot be read or decrypted.
If the AEC believes a Tender to potentially contain any Harmful Code or anything else that might compromise the integrity or security of AusTender and/or the AEC’s ICT environment, it may also be excluded from consideration.
Proof of electronic lodgement
When a Tender lodgement has successfully completed, an official receipt is provided on-screen. The on-screen receipt will record the time and date the Tender was received by AusTender and will be conclusive evidence of successful lodgement of a Tender. It is essential that Tenderers save and print this receipt as proof of lodgement. A separate email confirming receipt of the Tender will also be automatically dispatched to the email address of the registered user whose details were recorded at login.
Failure to receive a receipt means that lodgement has not completed successfully. Where no receipt has been issued by AusTender, the attempted lodgement will be deemed to have been unsuccessful. Tenderers should refer to clauses 5.4(a) and 5.4(d). 
AusTender security
Tenderers acknowledge that although the Commonwealth has implemented the security measures described on AusTender, the Commonwealth does not warrant that unauthorised access to information and data transmitted via the internet will not occur.
Tenderers acknowledge that:
lodgement of their Tender on time and in accordance with this RFT is entirely their responsibility; and
the AEC will not be liable for any loss, damage, costs or expenses incurred by Tenderers or any other person if, for any reason, a Tender or any other material or communication relevant to this RFT is not received on time, is corrupted or altered or otherwise is not received as sent, cannot be read or decrypted, or has its security or integrity compromised.
It is the responsibility of the Tenderers to ensure that their infrastructure including operating system and browser revision levels meet the minimum standards as defined on AusTender. Neither the AEC nor the Commonwealth take any responsibility for any problems arising from Tenderers’ infrastructure and/or internet connectivity.
Corrections and additions
Tenders containing pricing or other information that is not stated clearly and legibly may be excluded from consideration.
If a Tenderer becomes aware of any discrepancy, error, ambiguity, inconsistency or omission in its Tender prior to the RFT Closing Time and the Tenderer wishes to lodge a correction, that material must be in writing and lodged in accordance with clause 5.2(a).
Subject to clauses 9.4 and 9.9, if correcting information is delivered after the RFT Closing Time, the AEC may treat it as a Late Tender.
Unintentional errors of form
If the AEC considers that there is an unintentional error of form in a Tender, the AEC may, at its absolute discretion, allow the Tenderer to correct or clarify the error.
Where the AEC considers that such a correction or clarification would introduce unfairness into the evaluation process, the corrections will not be admitted for evaluation.
Clarification of Tenders by the AEC
After the RFT Closing Time, the AEC may seek clarification on any matter from any Tenderer.
The AEC may require a Tenderer to submit additional information to allow further consideration of its Tender. 
In the event that the AEC requires clarification of a Tender, the Tenderer may be required to courier or security post the originals of the signature and/or initialled pages to the AEC within the timeframe and to the address specified by the AEC.    
Tenderers should:
respond to any request for clarification within the time period and in the format specified by the AEC;
ensure that clarifying information provided answers the AEC's enquiry and is consistent with the Tender submitted by the Tenderer; and
not seek to materially alter any aspect of their Tender.
Where the AEC considers that such clarification or additional information would introduce unfairness into the evaluation process, it will not be admitted for evaluation.
Evaluation 
Overview
Following the RFT Closing Time, an evaluation process will take place to determine the Tenders that offer the best value for money overall for the AEC.  
The evaluation process will be conducted in the following stages:
Stage 1: Initial screening;
Stage 2: Assessment against the Scored Evaluation Criteria; and
Stage 3: Assessment of Best Value for Money Overall.
Neither the Tenderer with the lowest price, nor any Tender, will necessarily be accepted by the AEC.
Evaluation Criteria
The Evaluation Criteria are listed in Item 12. They are provided for the information of Tenderers to enable them to prepare a Tender that best satisfies this RFT.
Each Tenderer is encouraged to address the Evaluation Criteria in its Tender in sufficient detail to enable an effective evaluation of its Tender. 
The Evaluation Criteria are not listed in any order of importance, however, a Scored Evaluation Criterion identified as Important will have greater weight than one identified as Desirable. If weightings are to be used, the specific weighting of each criterion will be set out in Item 12 if required by the CPRs.
In relation to the Non-scored Evaluation Criteria:
Tenderers should complete all Annexures in Schedule 3;
the details included in the Pricing Schedule should cover all costs of providing the Services in accordance with the Deed of Standing Offer and Statement of Requirements, including any operating margin, finance cost and return on equity;
other than in the Pricing Schedule, price details, such as surcharges, transport cost pricing, among others, should not be included anywhere else in the Tender;
competitive neutrality requires that government business activities should not enjoy net competitive advantages over their private sector competitors simply by virtue of public sector ownership.  Each Tenderer from the public sector (if any) should demonstrate in its price that the requirements of competitive neutrality have been met, including payment of relevant taxes and charges, rates of return and costs of funds; and
Tenderers should include all assumptions or other caveats on which Tenders are based, and any effect of the assumptions or other caveats on price, as well as information or events required to remove caveats in the Tenderer's completed Pricing Schedule.  If no assumptions or other caveats are included in the Tenderer's completed Pricing Schedule, the Tenderer will be deemed to have represented to the AEC that no assumptions or other caveats apply.
Evaluation approach
Tenderers should note that the AEC intends to select Preferred Tenderers (if any) primarily based on the written Tenders submitted. Tenderers should not consider the AEC to have any prior or assumed knowledge regarding the Tenderer or their capacity to deliver the Services. Accordingly, Tenderers are encouraged to submit their best and unconditional offer in the first instance. 
Each Tender that is complete and meets the Mandatory Requirements will be evaluated to determine how well it meets each Evaluation Criterion.
Tenderers should note that in evaluating a Tender against each Evaluation Criterion, the AEC will consider the entirety of the Tenderer's Tender.  
The evaluation process may involve, at any stage and at the sole and absolute discretion of the AEC:
the AEC contacting some or all Tenderers to clarify aspects of their Tenders;
shortlisting;
presentations or interviews with Tenderers or visits to some or all Tenderers' sites;
discussions with, and/or visits to some or all Tenderers or their customers or subcontractors, whether or not those persons are provided as referees by a Tenderer; and/or
AEC negotiations with any or all Tenderers (see clause 7).
The AEC may make independent enquiries about any matter that may be relevant to the evaluation of a Tender including in relation to performance of any other contract the Tenderer has had with the AEC or any other Commonwealth entity.
The AEC may also include an assessment of risk to the AEC in general and to this RFT process, in particular in its evaluation against each Evaluation Criterion (as well as in the Assessment of Best Value for Money Overall).  The Tenderer's Compliance Statement will be taken into account in the assessment of risk.
Stage 1 – Initial screening 
At Stage 1, the AEC will undertake an initial assessment as to whether each Tender:
is a Late Tender;
complies with each of the Mandatory Requirements;
contains any unintentional errors of form; and
is otherwise incomplete or non compliant with this RFT.
Late Tenders will be excluded from consideration in accordance with clause 5.5. 
Subject to clause 6.4(d), Tenders which the AEC determines do not comply with each of the Mandatory Requirements will be excluded from further consideration.
Where the AEC determines that a Tender is incomplete or does contain unintentional errors of form, the AEC may request the Tenderer to correct or clarify the error in accordance with clause 5.10.
Where the AEC determines that a Tender is otherwise incomplete or non-compliant with this RFT, the AEC may exclude that Tender from further consideration.
The AEC may reach the view that Tenders fall within the categories listed in clauses 6.4(c), 6.4(d) or 6.4(e) at any stage during the evaluation process. 
Stage 2 – Assessment against the Scored Evaluation Criteria
The AEC will assess Tenders that have progressed to Stage 2 against the Scored Evaluation Criteria. 
In evaluating Tenders against the Scored Evaluation Criteria, the AEC will consider:
the merit of the Tender and how it conforms (including completeness) to the relevant Scored Evaluation Criterion;
the degree with which the Tenderer’s assertions and claims are demonstrated or supported, and the merit of any supporting information provided; and
the weighting of each criterion (if applicable).
Stage 3 – Assessment of Best Value for Money Overall
The AEC will determine which of the Tenderers (if any) are likely to be able to provide the Services to the AEC at the best value for money overall, through a comparative assessment of the Tenders against the:   
outcome of Stage 2; and
Non-scored Evaluation Criteria.
When assessing Tenders against the Non-scored Evaluation Criteria:
the AEC will conduct a financial assessment of the prices provided in the completed Pricing Schedule;
the AEC may, at its absolute discretion, consider and if necessary adjust prices in order to establish a common basis for the comparison of Tenders. Such adjustments may include but are not limited to:
consideration of normalised and discounted cash flow;
cost of administration of the Deed of Standing Offer (and any Agreements under it);
any assumptions or other caveats attaching to the price;
consideration of alternative proposals or financial incentives offered by a Tenderer;
accounting for pricing flexibility;
implementation and transition-out costs;
analysis of risks related to a Tender; and
other costs, if any, or financial impacts (including discounts) on the AEC that may arise from appointing a particular Tenderer; 
the AEC may use discounted cash flow to estimate the net present value of amounts in future years of the Deed of Standing Offer (or any Agreements under it), with all assumptions on costs, interest rates and related factors to be determined solely at the discretion of the AEC; 
the AEC is obliged to satisfy itself that prices offered are reasonable. Tenderers agree to provide access to such information as determined by the AEC in order to evaluate the reasonableness of their prices; and
the AEC may, without limitation, take into account:
the Tenderer's response to all Annexures in Schedule 3;
risks identified as a result of the assessment of the Tender against the other Evaluation Criteria;
risks identified from sources other than the Tenderer; 
risks identified as part of conducting the overall risk assessment; and
other risks identified during the evaluation of each Tender.
Negotiation 
Negotiation process
One or more Preferred Tenderers may be chosen for negotiations.
During the negotiation phase (if any) of this RFT process, the AEC may:
engage in detailed discussions and negotiations, including parallel negotiations, with the goal of maximising the value for money overall of a Tender to the AEC; and
seek variations to an offer or may seek supplementary offers in respect of any changes to the originally stated requirements.
As part of the negotiation phase, Preferred Tenderers may be asked to improve any or all aspects of their Tender.
The selection of the Service Provider(s) will be subject to the successful conclusion of negotiations and entry into a Deed of Standing Offer.  
Conduct of negotiations
During negotiations, Tenderers must not seek to alter a clause in the draft Deed of Standing Offer to which they have stated they comply in their Compliance Statement.
Without limiting its other rights under this RFT, if in the AEC's view, during negotiations a Preferred Tenderer has retracted, or attempts to retract, agreements under which material business, financial, technical and / or legal issues were resolved or agreed, the AEC may:
reject the Preferred Tenderer's Tender;
discontinue negotiations with that Tenderer;
re-enter negotiations or parallel negotiations with other Tenderers (including or excluding the Preferred Tenderer and other Preferred Tenderers); 
terminate this RFT process; and/or
exercise any other right reserved to the AEC under law or elsewhere in this RFT.
Notification of outcome and debriefing
The RFT process concludes when one of the following occurs:
the AEC enters into a Deed of Standing Offer with one or more Tenderers; or
the AEC terminates this RFT process in accordance with clause 9.12(a)(xvi).
At the conclusion of the RFT process, the AEC will:
notify all unsuccessful Tenderers who submitted a Tender in writing of the outcome of the evaluation of their Tender; and
offer all Tenderers the opportunity for a debrief in relation to their Tender.
Other terms and conditions
Ownership of Tender documents
The AEC will retain all documents lodged in response to this RFT to enable the evaluation of Tenders and to comply with obligations relating to accountability and record keeping.  Accordingly, Tenderers lodge all material in response to this RFT on the basis that they will become the physical property of the AEC on submission.
Ownership of Intellectual Property Rights in RFT and Tender
Such Intellectual Property Rights as may exist in the Information will remain the property of the AEC. The Tenderer is permitted to use that Information for the purpose only of compiling its Tender and, in the case of the short listed Tenderers, for subsequently negotiating the terms of the Deed of Standing Offer with the AEC.
Such Intellectual Property Rights as may exist in a Tender will remain the property of the Tenderer. Each Tenderer licenses the AEC, its officers, employees, agents, advisers and Ministers and other government representatives to copy, adapt, modify, disclose or do anything else necessary (in the AEC's sole opinion) to all material (including that which contains Intellectual Property Rights of the Tenderer or any other person) contained in that Tender for the purposes of:
conducting this procurement process including evaluating and clarifying Tenders and managing any resulting Deed of Standing Offer with a successful Tenderer; 
complying with any law; and
anything else related to the above purposes, including 
audits; 
complying with governmental and parliamentary reporting requirements including reporting to the Minister;
responding to requests for information from parliament or parliamentary committees; and
responding to any dispute regarding this RFT process.
Tender Validity Period
Each Tender, including the price, remains valid for consideration and acceptance by the AEC throughout the Tender Validity Period.
Political neutrality and Conflict of Interest
Note to Tenderers:  Conflict of Interest is defined to include circumstances where direct or indirect relationship or interest the Tenderer or its personnel may be seen to compromise the political neutrality of the AEC.
The AEC requires that its contractors:  
respect the strict political neutrality of the AEC, as it relates to AEC’s performance of its functions under the Commonwealth Electoral Act 1918 (Cth), the Referendum (Machinery Provisions) Act 1984 (Cth), Fair Work (Registered Organisations) Act 2009 (Cth) and the Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Act 2005 (Cth); and
do not associate the AEC in any way with any political activity that they undertake (including in relation to a plebiscite or referendum).
Where contractors provide personnel to work under the direction of the AEC in performing services, those personnel may be required to sign a declaration of political neutrality in such a form as may be required by the AEC to ensure that such personnel are aware of this requirement.
The Tenderer should declare at Annexure M to Schedule 3 any circumstances or relationships that constitute or may constitute an actual or potential Conflict of Interest in respect of this RFT or the Tenderer's obligations under any Deed of Standing Offer arising from this RFT process.
If a Tenderer declares a Conflict of Interest pursuant to clause 9.4(c), the Tenderer should set out at Annexure M to Schedule 3 its proposed strategy for managing each such Conflict of Interest.
If at any time prior to entering into a Deed of Standing Offer, an actual or potential Conflict of Interest arises or may arise for any Tenderer, that Tenderer should immediately notify the AEC in writing of that Conflict of Interest. The AEC may, at its absolute discretion:
enter into discussions to seek to resolve such Conflict of Interest;
disregard the Tender submitted by that Tenderer; or
take any other action as it considers appropriate, at its absolute discretion.
Tenderer conduct
The AEC's policy is to engage in the highest standards of ethical behaviour and fair dealing throughout this RFT process. The AEC requires the same standards from those with whom it deals. 
Tenders must not be prepared with:
improper assistance of employees or former employees, or contractors or former contractors of the AEC; or
the use of information improperly obtained or in breach of an obligation of confidentiality.
A Tenderer must not:
engage in misleading or deceptive conduct in connection with this RFT process;
engage in any collusive tendering, anti-competitive conduct, or any other unlawful or unethical conduct with any other Tenderer, or any other person in connection with this RFT process; or
attempt to influence improperly any officer, employee or agent of the AEC, or violate any applicable laws or AEC policies regarding the offering of inducements in connection with this RFT process. 
The AEC will exclude from consideration any Tender lodged by a Tenderer who, in the AECs reasonable opinion, has engaged in any behaviour contrary to this clause 9.5.
Due diligence
Tenderers will be expected to undertake an examination of all matters relevant to the provision of the Services to the AEC prior to lodging their Tender. Tenderers will be deemed to have made their own inquiries regarding provision of the Services. 
The AEC may at its sole and absolute discretion exclude from consideration any Tender that is made subject to any due diligence or other investigation to be performed after the Tender is submitted.
Return of Information
The AEC may, at its absolute discretion, require that at any stage all written Information provided to Tenderers (whether confidential or otherwise and without regard to the type of media on which such Information was provided to any Tenderer), and all copies of such Information made by Tenderers, be:
returned to the AEC - in which case the Tenderer must promptly return all such Information to the address identified by the AEC; or
destroyed by the Tenderer - in which case the Tenderer must promptly destroy all such Information and provide the AEC with written certification that the Information has been destroyed.
Confidentiality of Tenderer information
The AEC will, subject to this RFT, endeavour to treat the following information as confidential:
all Tenders received prior to entering into a Deed of Standing Offer;
all unsuccessful Tenders, following entry into a Deed of Standing Offer; and
all successful Tenders, following entry into a Deed of Standing Offer but only to the extent that:
the successful Tenderer requests that specific information in that Tender be kept confidential; and
the AEC has determined that specific information is to be kept confidential in accordance with the Confidentiality Test and has agreed, pursuant to the Deed of Standing Offer with the successful Tenderer, to keep that information confidential.
Each Tenderer should identify in Annexure L to Schedule 3 any information that it is providing to the AEC in, or related to, its Tender that it considers should be subject to an obligation of confidence on the part of the AEC in the event a Deed of Standing Offer is entered into.  
The AEC will not be taken to have breached any obligation to keep information provided by Tenderers confidential to the extent that the information is:
disclosed by the AEC to its advisers, officers, employees or subcontractors solely in order to conduct this procurement process or to prepare and manage any resultant Deed of Standing Offer (or any Agreement under it);
disclosed to the AEC’s internal management personnel to enable effective management or auditing of this procurement process;
disclosed by the AEC to the responsible Minister or in response to a request by a House or a Committee of the Parliament of the Commonwealth of Australia;
shared by the AEC within the AEC’s organisation, or with another Commonwealth agency, where this serves the Commonwealth’s legitimate interests;
authorised or required by law to be disclosed; 
disclosed to meet the AEC’s reporting or accountability requirements; 
disclosed by the AEC to the Auditor General, Ombudsman, Privacy Commissioner, Information Commissioner or Freedom of Information Commissioner or their delegates; or
in the public domain otherwise than due to a breach of the relevant obligations of confidentiality.
Change affecting Tenderer
A Tenderer must promptly notify the AEC of any change, after submission of its Tender, which may have an adverse impact on its access to the necessary skills, resources, or corporate or financial backing to perform the Services under the Deed of Standing Offer.
Where such change would alter any of the information or assurances that the Tenderer has given in its Tender, the Tenderer must promptly:
notify the AEC;
identify with specificity (including any relevant page, section, clause, Schedule, Annexure and other like references to its Tender, and any other material and information provided to the AEC) all such information and assurances; and
state in detail the alterations to such information and assurances required by such change.
If the AEC receives a notice under clauses 9.9(a) or (b), or becomes aware of an event under those clauses, the AEC may either exclude the Tender from consideration or consider the Tender taking into account the impact of the changed circumstances on the information provided in the Tender.
Publicity
The Tenderer may not issue any information, publication, document or article for publication in any media which includes details of the Services under the Deed of Standing Offer or which otherwise refers or is connected to the AEC or this RFT process, without the prior written approval of the AEC. 
No contract or undertaking
Without limiting any obligations on a Tenderer, nothing in this RFT will be construed to create any binding contract (express or implied) between the AEC and any Tenderer until a Deed of Standing Offer is entered into with the successful Tenderer (if any).  Any conduct or statement whether prior to or subsequent to the issue of this RFT is not, and this RFT is not, and must not be deemed to be:
an offer to contract by the AEC; or
a binding undertaking of any kind by the AEC (including, without limitation, quasi-contractual rights, promissory estoppel, or rights with a similar legal basis).
If a court finds there to be a contract between the AEC and a Tenderer regarding the conduct of this RFT process contrary to clause 9.11(a), the AEC's liability for any breach of the terms of such contract is limited to the Tenderer's direct substantiated costs of participation in this RFT process. The AEC's liability generally in connection with this RFT process (eg for negligence or breach of statute) is limited to the same amount. For the avoidance of doubt, the AEC will not be liable for any Indirect Loss of the Tenderer.
AEC's rights
Without limiting its rights at law or otherwise and notwithstanding any other clause of this RFT, the AEC reserves the right at its absolute discretion at any time to:
vary this RFT process;
provide or not provide additional clarification or information to Tenderers;
amend this RFT, including extending the RFT Closing Time, by giving written notice to Tenderers (where possible) and advertising the changes on the AusTender website;
issue addenda to this RFT, in which case all terms and conditions of this RFT will apply to any addenda unless amended in the addenda;
amend the terms and conditions or the schedules of the Deed of Standing Offer at any time, including:
to add to, alter, delete or exclude any part of the Services to be supplied under the Deed of Standing Offer; and
following the receipt and evaluation of Tenders;
require additional information or clarification from any Tenderer or anyone else or provide additional information or clarification;
negotiate with any one or more Tenderers (including without shortlisting), or discontinue negotiations with any Tenderer at any time for any reason;
allow any Tenderer to change its Tender at any time;
seek amended, or call for new, Tenders;
reject any Tender received after the RFT Closing Time;
shortlist or select Preferred Tenderers or choose not to do so;
accept all or part of any Tender, including a non-compliant Tender or a Tender that is not the lowest priced;
reject all or part of a Tender at any time, including a Tender that complies with this RFT;
accept a Tender for a different requirement than set out in the Statement of Requirements;
reject or accept at any time any alternative Tender;
cease to proceed with, or suspend, the process outlined in this RFT, including where the AEC considers that:
it is in the public interest to do so;
no Tender meets the Mandatory Requirements;
no Tenderer is fully capable of undertaking the Deed of Standing Offer; 
the Australian Government approves the procurement of the Services under a co-ordinated procurement process as described in clause 10.2(a); or
no Tender represents value for money;
where clause 9.12(a)(xvi) applies:
negotiate with any person (including a person who is not a Tenderer) and enter into a Deed of Standing Offer for the provision of some of all of the Services with that person on such terms as the AEC at its absolute discretion accepts without prior notice to any Tenderer;
enter into an agreement or binding relationship outside the RFT process; or
not award an agreement at all;
publish or disclose the names of the successful Tenderer(s) and the subcontractors to be used in supplying the Services; 
allow or not allow a Related Body Corporate or another legal entity to take over a Tender in substitution for the original Tenderer; or
if a panel of Service Providers is established, cancel, vary, supplement, supersede or replace the panel or any member of the panel.
Any time or date contained in this RFT is provided for the sole convenience of the AEC. The establishment of a time or date in this RFT does not create an obligation on the part of the AEC to take any action or create any right in any Tenderer that any action be taken on the date established. The AEC may, but is not obliged to, notify affected Tenderers if the AEC exercises any of its rights listed above in clause 9.12(a), but will not be obliged to provide any reasons for its actions.

it has satisfied itself as to the correctness and sufficiency of its Tender; 
in deciding whether to submit a Tender, it has relied entirely on its own enquiries and has not relied on any express or implied statement, warranty or representation, whether oral, written, or otherwise made by or on behalf of the AEC, its officers, employees, agents or advisers other than any statement, warranty or representation contained in Information made available in writing by the AEC;

Participation in any stage of this RFT process, or in relation to any matter concerning this RFT will be at each Tenderer's sole risk, cost and expense.  The Tenderer will be and the AEC will not be responsible in any circumstances for any costs or expenses incurred by any Tenderer related to:
responding to this RFT, or taking any action in relation to this RFT process; 
any Deed of Standing Offer negotiations;
any other action including any dispute or response in relation to this RFT; or
any agreement a Tenderer has made or may make in connection with any potential Deed of Standing Offer.   
Without limiting clause 9.14(a), the AEC is not liable to any Tenderer on the basis of any promissory estoppel, quantum meruit, quantum valebat, or any other contractual, quasi contractual or restitutionary grounds or in negligence as a consequence of any matter or thing relating to or incidental to a Tenderer's participation in this RFT process, including, without limitation:
the procurement process for this RFT;
where a Tenderer is not successful under this RFT process;
where the AEC varies, suspends or terminates this RFT process or negotiates with a Tenderer; or
where the AEC exercises, or fails to exercise, any of its other rights under or in relation to this RFT.
Applicable law
The law applying in the Australian Capital Territory applies to this RFT and this RFT process.  Each Tenderer must comply with all relevant laws in preparing and lodging its Tender and taking part in this RFT process.
Complaints
If a Tenderer has a complaint concerning this RFT or the AEC's management of any aspect of this RFT process, the Tenderer should provide written notice of the details of the complaint:
where the complaint is about an alleged contravention of the Commonwealth Procurement Rules under the Government Procurement (Judicial Review) Act 2018 (Cth) (GPJR Act) with the accountably authority (or its delegate) of the AEC by submitting a complaint via the AEC website; and
for all other complaints -  to the Contact Officer.
A complaint lodged by the Tenderer under clause 9.16(a)(i), should comply with the requirements under the GPJR Act and must detail:  
any alleged or proposed conduct in contravention of the relevant Commonwealth Procurement Rules; and
how the interests of the Tenderer or a supplier are affected by the alleged or proposed conduct. 
If the AEC receives a complaint lodged by a Tenderer under clause 9.16(a)(i) then the AEC reserves the right to suspend this RFT process as required under the GPJR Act. 
The AEC will provide acknowledgement to the Tenderer that the complaint has been received in accordance with the AEC Complaint Management Policy.
Complaints will not prejudice a Tenderer’s participation in any of the AEC’s future RFT processes. 
Applicable laws and policies
The attention of each Tenderer is drawn to the legislation and policies set out in this clause 10.  The Tenderer should obtain, and will be deemed to have obtained, its own advice on the impact of any laws and policies on its participation in this RFT process and performance of the Deed of Standing Offer.
Commonwealth procurement
This procurement process will be conducted in accordance with the Commonwealth Procurement Rules (CPRs).  
Tenderers should make their own enquiries about the CPRs and other relevant Commonwealth policies which apply to the procurement process initiated by this RFT.  The website of the Department of Finance is an appropriate starting point for any inquiry about relevant Commonwealth procurement policy - see https://www.finance.gov.au/government/procurement
Coordinated procurement
It is possible that the Australian Government may approve the procurement of some or all of the Services under a coordinated process:
before the Tender Validity Period ends; 
after the Tender Validity Period ends but before a Deed of Standing Offer is signed with the successful Tenderer(s); or 
during the term of any Deed of Standing Offer entered into as a result of this RFT.  
If clause 10.2(a)(i) or clause 10.2(a)(ii) applies, the AEC reserves the right to discontinue this RFT process. If clause 10.2(a)(iii) applies, the AEC may exercise its right under the Deed of Standing Offer to terminate the Deed of Standing Offer for convenience. 
Each Tenderer should refer to the Deed of Standing Offer, which details the termination mechanisms that may apply to the Deed of Standing Offer. Further information about coordinated procurement and scoping studies is available at the Department of Finance website.
Indigenous Procurement Policy
Tenderers should note that the Indigenous Procurement Policy applies to this procurement.  More information on the Indigenous Procurement Policy can be found at https://www.niaa.gov.au/sites/default/files/publications/indigenous-procurement-policy.pdf
Tenderers should note that while the Indigenous Procurement Policy applies, given the nature of this procurement, Section 3 (mandatory set-aside of certain Commonwealth contracts) and Section 4 (Minimum requirements for certain Commonwealth contracts) do not apply to this RFT or any resulting Agreement.  Notwithstanding this, the AEC welcomes responses to this RFT from Indigenous owned businesses.
Access and disclosure regimes
Rights of the Auditor-General
The Auditor-General Act 1997 (Cth) provides the Auditor-General or an authorised person with a right to have, at all reasonable times, access to information, documents and records.
Freedom of information
The Freedom of Information Act 1982 (Cth) and the Freedom of Information Amendment (Reform) Act 2010 (Cth) (together the FOl Act) give members of the public right of access to documents in the possession of the Commonwealth and its agencies. 
The FOI Act extends as far as possible the right of the community to access documents in the possession of the Commonwealth, limited only by exceptions and exemptions necessary for the protection of essential public interests and the private and business affairs of persons in respect of whom information is collected and held by agencies and public authorities.
Public interest disclosure
The Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) (the PID Act) applies to Australian Government agencies, Commonwealth companies, public authorities and Commonwealth contracted service providers.
The PID Act removes barriers that prevent people who work in the public sector from speaking up about serious problems. It also makes sure disclosures are properly investigated and provides protection for those that report allegations. 
Publication of Deed of Standing Offer details
Tenderers should be aware that the AEC is subject to specific accountability requirements, which support internal and external scrutiny of its tendering and contracting processes. These include:
the requirement to publish details of:
contracts and agreements with an estimated value of $10,000 or more; and 
standing offer arrangements regardless of value,
on AusTender;
the requirement to report details of Commonwealth contracts valued at $100,000 or more in accordance with the Procedural Order of Continuing Effect: Department and Agency Contracts, including the:
name of the service provider and the subject matter of the contract;
total value of the contract; and
whether the contract contains clauses that are confidential, and if so, the reasons for confidentiality; 
the requirement to publish information about certain procurements in annual reports; and
the requirement to make available, on request, the names of any subcontractors engaged to perform services in relation to the Deed of Standing Offer (as such, Tenderers must inform all potential subcontractors that their participation in fulfilling obligations under the Deed of Standing Offer may be publicly disclosed and obtain their agreement to this).
Workplace Gender Equality 
Australian Government policy prevents the Commonwealth from entering into contracts with suppliers who are currently named as non-compliant under the Workplace Gender Equality Act 2012 (Cth) (WGE Act). The Deed of Standing Offer requires that each Service Provider must:
comply with its obligations, if any, under the WGE Act; and 
if the term of the Deed of Standing Offer exceeds 18 months, the Service Provider must provide a current letter of compliance within 18 months from the Commencement Date and following this, annually to the AEC,
if they are a relevant employer as defined under the WGE Act.
Successful Tenderers should note that if during the term of the Deed of Standing Offer the Service Provider becomes non-compliant with the WGE Act, the Service Provider must notify the AEC. For further information about the coverage of the WGE Act, Tenderers can contact the Workplace Gender Equality Agency on (02) 9432 7000.
Tenderers must indicate as part of their Tender at Annexure B to Schedule 3, whether or not the Tenderer is a ‘relevant employer’ under the WGE Act and, if applicable provide a current letter of compliance with the WGE Act as part of their Tender, or prior to entering into a Deed of Standing Offer.
Environmental policy and procurement
The Australian Government aims to improve the implementation of ecologically sustainable development within its agencies. Copies of relevant policies, such as the National Waste policy, Energy Efficiency in Government Operations policy and the Australian Packaging Covenant, are available at the Department of the Environment’s website:  http://www.environment.gov.au/.
The AEC wishes to be informed of any claims made by Tenderers about the benefits to the environment of the Services including details of actions taken by the Tenderer to:
improve the energy / resource efficiency or reduce energy consumption associated with delivery of the Services;
reduce or eliminate waste associated with the delivery of the Services; and
reduce fuel use, reduce air pollution and mitigate emissions associated with transport.
Tenderers should indicate the particular part of the production process or characteristic of any part of the Services offered by them that they claim is environmentally beneficial in their Tender at Annexure G to Schedule 3.
Employee entitlements
It is Australian Government policy not to contract with suppliers who have had a judicial decision against them (not including decisions under appeal) relating to employee entitlements and who have not paid the claim.
To enable the AEC to confirm the Tenderer’s compliance with this requirement, each Tenderer must complete the Tenderer's Declaration.
Illegal workers
It is Australian Government policy not to contract with suppliers that engage illegal workers. 
Lobbying Code of Conduct
A person engaged as a contractor by the AEC will be a "government representative" for the purposes of the Lobbying Code of Conduct, and will be required to comply with the Lobbying Code of Conduct.  A copy of the Lobbying Code of Conduct is available at: https://lobbyists.ag.gov.au/about/code.
The Tenderer must, and must ensure that its subcontractors (if any) will, comply with the Lobbying Code of Conduct and the Australian Public Service Commission (APSC) Circular 2008/4, Requirements relating to the Lobbying Code of Conduct and Post Separation Contact with Government where their activities fall within the scope of the Lobbying Code of Conduct.
Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct
It is a condition of this tender that the Tenderer complies with the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct (ttps://www.finance.gov.au/government/procurement/commonwealth-supplier-code-conduct/commonwealth-supplier-code-conduct). 
To enable the AEC to confirm the Tenderer’s compliance with this requirement, each Tenderer must complete the Tenderer’s Declaration.

Background
The AEC has issued a Request for Tender AEC SRC006427 for the provision of furniture, warehousing and other equipment for Electoral Events (RFT).
As part of the RFT process, the AEC requires the Tenderer to provide this Tenderer's Declaration. 
Operative provisions
Authority
The Representative holds the position of [managing director or other title] of the Tenderer and is duly authorised to execute this deed on the Tenderer’s behalf.
The Representative executes this deed on behalf of the Tenderer and on behalf of itself. 
Definitions and interpretation
In this deed:
Tender means the tender lodged by the Tenderer in connection with the RFT;
Tenderer means [list name, ABN and physical address of Tenderer] and in this deed includes the Representative; and
all other capitalised terms have the same meaning as in the RFT.
In this deed headings are for convenience only and do not affect interpretation and unless the context indicates a contrary intention:
a reference to a party includes that party's executors, administrators, successors and that party's permitted assigns, including persons taking by way of novation;
a reference to a part, clause, schedule, exhibit, attachment or annexure is a reference to a clause, schedule, exhibit, attachment or annexure to or of this deed poll;
a reference to an Annexure is a reference to an Annexure to the Tenderer's Tender Form;
a reference to a document (including this deed poll) is to that document as varied, novated, ratified or replaced from time to time;
a word importing the singular includes the plural (and vice versa), and a word indicating a gender includes every other gender;
if a word or phrase is given a defined meaning, any other part of speech or grammatical form of that word or phrase has a corresponding meaning; and
the word "includes" in any form is not a word of limitation.
The offer and change of circumstances
To the best of the Tenderer's knowledge after having made proper enquiry, the Tender is accurate in every respect. The Tenderer acknowledges that if it is found to have made false or misleading claims or statements, or to have used Confidential Information improperly obtained, or received improper assistance, except in accordance with the RFT, the AEC may reject at any time any Tender lodged by or on behalf of the Tenderer.
The Tenderer agrees:
that the Tenderer will be bound by its Tender for the Tender Validity Period;
that its Tender may be accepted by the AEC at any time before the expiration of the Tender Validity Period or as agreed between the Tenderer and the AEC;
that its Tender will not be deemed to be accepted except as specified in the RFT;
that the AEC is not bound to accept the lowest priced or any Tender; and
to promptly notify the AEC of any change, after submission of its Tender, to the basis upon which it will have access to the necessary skills, resources or corporate or financial backing to perform the Deed of Standing Offer.
Where a change referred to in clause 9.9 of the RFT would alter any of the information or assurances that the Tenderer has given in its Tender, the Tenderer undertakes to promptly:
notify the AEC;
identify with specificity (including all relevant page, section, clause, Schedule, exhibit and other like references to its Tender, and any other material and information provided to the AEC) all such information and assurances; and
state in detail the alterations to such information and assurances required by such change.
Tenderer's conduct
The Tenderer represents that, to the best of its knowledge after having made proper enquiry, it has:
not made any false declarations in respect of any current or past dealings with the AEC, including in any procurement process or in any contract for goods, services or construction works;
had no significant deficiency in the performance of any substantive requirement or obligation under any prior contract with the AEC which would materially adversely affect the Tenderer’s ability to deliver the Services as required;
the skills and resources (including, without limitation, corporate and financial resources), or will be able to obtain the skills and resources, likely to be required to properly provide the Services; 
not, in connection with the RFT process:
engaged in misleading or deceptive conduct; or
engaged in any collusive tendering, anti-competitive conduct, or any other unlawful or unethical conduct with any other Tenderer, or any other person;
examined the RFT and any other information made available to it in writing by the AEC for the purpose of this RFT;
examined all further information which is obtainable by making reasonable enquiries relevant to the risks, contingencies and other circumstances having an effect on its Tender; and
satisfied itself as to the correctness and sufficiency of its Tender.
The Tenderer has not attempted to influence improperly any officer, employee or agent of the AEC, or violated any applicable laws or the AEC policies regarding the offering of inducements in connection with the RFT process.
The Tenderer has not prepared its Tender with the improper assistance of employees or former employees, or any contractor or former contractors of the AEC or with the use of information improperly obtained or in breach of an obligation of confidentiality.
Neither the Tenderer nor any of its employees, agents or contractors had knowledge of the content of any Tender (including price) of any other Tenderer prior to the Tenderer submitting its Tender.
Neither the Tenderer nor any of its employees, agents or contractors disclosed the content of the Tender (including price) submitted by the Tenderer to any other Tenderer or to any other person or organisation, excluding any subcontractors listed at Annexure A of the Tenderer’s Tender, prior to the RFT Closing Time.
Prior to the Tenderer submitting its Tender, neither the Tenderer nor any of its employees, agents or contractors entered into any contract, agreement, arrangement or understanding that if successful, the Tenderer would pay any money, or would provide any other benefit or other financial advantage, to or for the benefit of any other party who unsuccessfully responded to the RFT.
If the Tender is lodged via AusTender the Tenderer warrants that it has taken reasonable steps to ensure that the Tender files are free from any Harmful Code which may affect AusTender or the AEC's computing environment.

– Compliance Statement
Each Tenderer is to state in its Tender whether it complies with each clause of this RFT, including clauses in the Statement of Requirements at Schedule 1 and clauses in the draft Deed of Standing Offer at Schedule 2.   
A set of tables is provided below for the Tenderer to indicate its responses. The Tenderer should cross-refer to the RFT, Deed of Standing Offer and Statement of Requirements and indicate in the appropriate column, by way of a tick (P) whether the Tenderer fully complies, partially complies or does not comply with each provision.  
Where the Tenderer only partially complies or does not comply, the Tenderer should indicate the reason for, and extent of, non-compliance and propose any alternate wording for the relevant provision. The Tenderer should also provide reasons for any alternative wording.
The expressions used have the following meaning:
"complies" means:
in the case of a clause which imposes a contractual condition, that the condition is agreed to;
in the case of a clause which specifies a characteristic or performance to be met by the Tenderer, that the Tenderer can (and will) meet the requirement as specified;
in the case of a clause which is of an informative nature only, the clause has been read, understood and is agreed; or
in the case of a clause where information has been requested, that the information has been provided in the required level of detail and in the required format;
"does not comply" means that the contractual obligation, characteristic or performance requirement of the clause cannot be met by the Tenderer; and
"partially complies" means that the contractual condition, characteristic or performance requirement of the clause can be substantially met, subject to certain specified qualifications.
The Tenderer can respond by stating that it complies with all or a group of clauses and paragraphs and note its response on an exceptions basis by separately identifying the clauses or paragraphs with which it partially complies or does not comply.  
The AEC will consider the Tenderer's Compliance Statement in its evaluation of Tenders in accordance with the RFT.  If the Tenderer fails to respond to this Schedule and does not clearly mitigate its risk to the AEC elsewhere in its Tender, its Tender may be evaluated as a high risk to AEC.
The AEC does not intend to negotiate in relation to any clause or schedule in the draft Deed of Standing Offer if the Tenderer's Compliance Statement concerning that clause or schedule contains a response of "complies".

– Understanding of and approach to the Services
Tenderer’s approach, clearly demonstrating their capacity and capability to deliver and invoice the Services.
Tenderers must provide detailed information on their proposed solution to the delivery of the Services set out in the Statement of Requirements, including: What processes does the Tenderer have in place to ensure they will be able to supply the quantity and types of materials they are offering, with the required specifications (see pricing schedule photos and descriptions) in the required timeframes?
In addition to your general model for providing the services, how will you specifically consider the following:
Products - What categories of items does the Tenderer proposing to supply? Does it cover all the item categories (e.g. the various furniture items and warehouse operational items such as shelving, pallet jacks, etc.)?
Types of operation - What categories of our operations does the Tenderer propose to supply? Does it cover OPCs, PPVCs and Static polling places?
Geographic coverage - Will the Tenderer service all identified areas/divisions, including rural and remote locations?  What logistics arrangements, distribution centres, and delivery contingencies support this?
In replying, please also elaborate how they have accounted for the fact that their services may not be required for months, or years (i.e. how can the Tenderer ensure stock is available, when required at an uncertain date in the future)?
How does the Tenderer propose to meet the reporting requirements in Item 2.2 of the Statement of Requirements?
In replying, please also elaborate on what measures will be proposed to make the AEC aware of any additional transport costs or surcharges, in advance, and how they intend to provide the AEC with viable options to lessen or avoid such charges, while still meeting agreed delivery and pick up timeframes?
The AEC has previously experienced difficulties reconciling invoice charges to activities, particularly transport charges and surcharges. As such, in addition to any information provided to earlier questions, please provide further specific detail to the following:
Provide an example of the type of document that will be provided to satisfy the invoicing requirements in Item 2.3 of the Statement of Requirements.  
How will they ensure their pricing structure will be transparent to allow reconciliation between services delivered and the agreed pricing structure (including, in addition to equipment costs, all transport & handling costs and all surcharges)?
Noting the answers to the above, are there any other matters not already mentioned that addresses how the Tenderer would propose to meet the Service Levels in Item 3 of the Statement of Requirements?

– Indicative Delivery Timings
As noted in Annexure D, the excel spreadsheet provided as an attachment contains the most up-to-date estimation of site locations and possible volumes.

Static Delivery Timings in the two weeks prior to polling day – Indicative representation from the 2025 Federal Election
The three indicative delivery timings for statics graphs from three different states shown below provide a general representation of the spread of deliveries in the two weeks prior to polling day. 
The following should be noted:
Polling day for the 2025 federal election was 3 May.
ANZAC Day intervened in the last week prior to polling (hence no deliveries on Friday 25 April).
These graphs are not a representation of actual furniture-only deliveries. They are an indication of when materials, including furniture, were delivered to static polling places (i.e. an indication of the availability of polling place venues for materials and furniture arrivals).
The ‘x’ axis (left hand side) indicates the percentage of total deliveries for that given day made to statics out of 100%. That is, a figure of 15% for a given day indicates the 15% of the statics (in number, not in volume of materials) were delivered on that day out of 100% (with 100% indicating the total sum of statics delivered to).

 Agreed Terms	
Part 1 - Deed
Definitions and interpretation
Definitions
In this Deed and any Agreement, except where the contrary intention is expressed, the following definitions are used:
Interpretation
In this Deed and any Agreement, except where the contrary intention is expressed:
the singular includes the plural and vice versa, and a gender includes other genders;
another grammatical form of a defined word or expression has a corresponding meaning;
a reference to a clause, paragraph, attachment or annexure is to a clause or paragraph of, or attachment or annexure to, this Deed;
a reference to a document or instrument includes the document or instrument as novated, altered, supplemented or replaced from time to time and any schedules, attachments, appendices or annexures to that document or instrument;
a reference to AUD, A$, $A, dollar or$ is to Australian currency;
a reference to time is to the time in the place where the obligation is to be performed;
a reference to a party is to a party to this Deed, and a reference to a party to a document includes the party's executors, administrators, successors and permitted assignees and substitutes;
a reference to a person includes a natural person, partnership, body corporate, association, governmental or local authority or agency or other entity;
if the Service Provider is a trustee, the Service Provider enters into this Deed personally and in its capacity as trustee and:
any warranties given under this Deed or an Agreement are given in both capacities; and
warrants that it has the power to perform its obligations under this Deed and any Agreement;
a reference to a statute, ordinance, code or other Law includes regulations and other instruments under it and consolidations, amendments, re-enactments or replacements of any of them;
a word or expression defined in the Corporations Act has the meaning given to it in the Corporations Act;
(I)	the meaning of general words is not limited by specific examples introduced by including, for example, or similar expressions;
any agreement, representation, warranty or indemnity by two or more parties (including where two or more persons are included in the same defined term) binds them jointly and severally;
any agreement, representation, warranty or indemnity in favour of two or more parties (including where two or more persons are included in the same defined term) is for the benefit of them jointly and severally;
a rule of construction does not apply to the disadvantage of a party because the party was responsible for the preparation of this Deed or a Work Order or any part of it;
if a day on which an obligation must be performed, or an event must occur by, is not a Business Day, the obligation must be performed on, or the event must occur by, the next Business Day; and
headings are for ease of reference only and do not affect interpretation.
Completion of Schedules
To the extent that the parties have not completed Items in a Schedule, unless otherwise stated in the Schedule or in this Deed, those Items will be taken to be 'not applicable' for the purpose of this Deed.

the Agreed Terms;
the Schedules in the order in which they appear;
any attachments to the Schedules in the order in which they appear; and
documents incorporated by reference in this Deed.

Ability to refuse Work Order or respond to an RFQ
On receipt of a Work Order under clause 4.3(a) or an RFQ under clause 4.5, the Service Provider may serve a Refusal Notice, notify the AEC that:
it is unable to perform against the Work Order; or
it is unable to substantively respond to an RFQ, due to a Refusal Reason.
Any Refusal Notice must be issued within the Refusal Period, being within
five Business Days of receipt of a Work Order or an RFQ or any shorter timeframe specified in the Work Order or RFQ.
Request for Quotation
The AEC may, from time to time, issue an RFQ substantially in the form of Schedule 6 prior to issuing a Work Order.
Information in RFQ
An RFQ will include:
the Services and/or Additional Services required;

the timeframe for delivery of the Services;
subject to clause 4.2, the proposed Fee structure for the Agreement; and
any other specific requirements of the AEC.
Quotations
On receipt of an RFQ, the Service Provider must, within the timeframe specified in the RFQ, provide the AEC with either:
a Refusal Notice stating that the Service Provider will not provide a Quotation due to a Refusal Reason; or
a Quotation by:
completing the appropriate Items in the RFQ and returning it to the AEC;
providing a brief statement that addresses the Evaluation Criteria (if applicable);
if requested by the AEC, providing a curriculum vitae for each of the Personnel proposed to perform the Services; and
if requested by the AEC, providing the contact details for referees.
If the AEC wishes to proceed to obtain the Services and/or Additional Services referred to in the Quotation, the AEC will issue a Work Order.
All Intellectual Property Rights in Quotations provided under clause 4.?(a)(ii) vest in the AEC on submission to the AEC. The AEC grants to the Service Provider a licence to use the Quotation on the same terms that AEC Material is licensed to the Service Provider under clause 18.2.

Subject to clause 4.8(b), an Agreement is formed between the AEC and the Service Provider when the AEC issues a Work Order to the Service Provider.
If the Service Provider issues a valid Refusal Notice within the Refusal Period, an Agreement formed under clause 4.8(a) is rescinded.
Completion of Items
To the extent that the parties have not completed Items in a Work Order, unless otherwise stated in the Work Order or this Deed, those Items will be taken to be 'not applicable' for the purpose of this Deed and an Agreement.
Terms and conditions of an Agreement
The terms and conditions of an Agreement are:
the terms and conditions specified in the signed Work Order;
clauses 1, 4.9, 4.10, 4.11 and 4.12 and clauses 10 to 24.3 of this Deed; and
clauses 26 to 43 of this Deed, except that a reference to 'this Deed' in those clauses is taken to be a reference to 'the Agreement' or 'an Agreement' and a reference to 'Deed Period' in those clauses is taken to be a reference to the 'Agreement Period'.
Priority of documents
Subject to clause 4.8, to the extent of any inconsistency between two or more documents which form part of an Agreement, those documents will be interpreted in the following (descending) order of priority:
the Work Order;
this Deed;
the attachments to the Work Order; and
any other document referred to in the Work Order.
