Termination of Deed
Termination for convenience
The AEC may, at any time, by notice, terminate this Deed, including for, or as a result of, a machinery of government change or the establishment of a whole of Australian Government panel or coordinated process for the Services.
On receipt of a notice of termination under clause 8.1(a) the Service Provider must:
stop work as specified in the notice; and
take all available steps to minimise loss resulting from that termination and to protect AEC Material.
If this Deed is terminated under clause 8.1(a), the AEC is liable only for reasonable costs actually incurred by the Service Provider and directly attributable to the termination.
The Service Provider is not entitled to compensation for loss of prospective profits or loss of opportunity.
Termination for default
Without limiting any other rights or remedies the AEC may have against the Service Provider arising out of or in connection with this Deed or any Agreement, the AEC may terminate this Deed effective immediately by giving notice to the Service Provider if:
the Service Provider breaches a material provision of this Deed where that breach is not capable of remedy;
the Service Provider breaches any provision of this Deed and fails to remedy the breach within 14 days after receiving notice requiring it to do so;
a Service Level Termination Trigger event occurs for reasons solely attributable to the Service Provider;
in the opinion of the AEC, a Conflict of Interest exists which would prevent the Service Provider from properly performing its obligations under this Deed; or
subject to the AEC complying with any requirements in the Corporations Act 2001 (Cth), an event specified in clause 8.2(c) happens to the Service Provider.
Without limitation, for the purposes of clause 8.2(a)(i), each of the following constitutes a breach of a material provision:
a breach of warranty under clause 12.2 (Service Provider warranties);
a failure to comply with clause 12.5(a)(ii) (workplace gender equality and subcontracting);
a failure to comply with clause 16 (Personnel);
a failure to comply with clause 18 (Intellectual Property Rights);
a failure to comply with clause 26 (Confidentiality and privacy);
a failure to comply with clause 27 (Protection of Personal Information);
a failure to comply with clause 30 (Political neutrality, Lobbying Code of Conduct and Conflict of Interest);
a failure to comply with clause 31 (Security);
a failure to comply with clause 34 (Workplace legislation);
a failure to comly with clause 37B (Supplier Code of Conduct); 
a failure to comply with clause 37C (Notification of Significant Events); oe
a failure to comply with clause 38 (Insurance).
The Service Provider must notify the AEC immediately if:
the Service Provider is a corporation and there is any change in the direct or indirect beneficial ownership or control of the Service Provider;
the Service Provider disposes of the whole or any part of its assets, operations or business other than in the ordinary course of business;
the Service Provider ceases to carry on business;
the Service Provider ceases to be able to pay its debts as and when they become due;
the Service Provider is a corporation and enters into liquidation or has a controller or managing controller or liquidator or administrator appointed;
the Service Provider is a natural person and is declared bankrupt or assigns his or her estate for the benefit of creditors; or
the Service Provider is a partnership and any step is taken to dissolve that partnership.
In this clause, controller, managing controller and administrator have the same meanings as in the Corporations Act.
After termination
On termination of this Deed the Service Provider must:
deal with AEC Material as reasonably directed by the AEC; and
return all of the AEC's Confidential Information to the AEC.
Termination does not affect accrued rights
Termination of this Deed does not affect any accrued rights or remedies of a party.
Termination does not affect Agreements
Termination of this Deed does not affect the continuance of any Agreement formed under this Deed.
Transition out
Transition out obligations
The Service Provider must provide the following assistance to the AEC on expiry or termination of this Deed:
cooperating with and providing all reasonable assistance and making available all required information to the AEC in support of the transition out of the Service Provider and the transition to the AEC or any new contractor of the provision of the Services;
doing all acts and things, and executing all documents as are reasonably necessary or desirable, in support of the transition out of the Service Provider;
replying to all and any requests for assistance as expediently as possible to ensure that the Service Provider does not hinder any procurement process or engagement or commencement of any new contractor;
ensuring seamless continuity of Services under each Agreement during the transition out period; and
making Personnel available for discussions with the AEC as may be required. The time, length and subject of these discussions will be at the sole discretion of the AEC, provided that any matter discussed is not considered to reveal any Confidential Information of the Service Provider.
Survival
This clause 9 survives the expiry or termination of this Deed.
Part 2 - Services
10.	Agreement Period
Agreement Period
The Agreement Period begins on the Services Start Date and continues until the Agreement End Date unless:
terminated in accordance with clause 24 or clause 39.3; or
extended in accordance with clause 10.2.

The Agreement Period may be extended by the AEC for the Agreement Option Period(s), on the terms and conditions then in effect, by giving a minimum of 30 day's notice to the Service Provider.
Any extension in accordance with this clause 10.2 takes effect from the end of the then current Agreement Period.
General obligations of the Service Provider
The Service Provider must, at all times:
act reasonably in performing its obligations and exercising its rights under the Agreement; and
diligently perform its obligations under the Agreement.
Provision of the Services
Service obligations
The Service Provider must supply the Services:
to the reasonable satisfaction of the AEC;
with due skill and care and to the best of the Service Provider's knowledge and expertise;
to a high standard and in accordance with the professional standards of conduct applying to the relevant industry;
to meet or exceed the Service Levels;
in accordance with best practice and guidelines, including any specified in the Work Order;
using the Specified Personnel (if any);
so as to keep the AEC fully and regularly informed as to all matters affecting or relating to the Services;
in an efficient, controlled and responsive manner, to a standard that at a minimum complies with this Deed;
continuously seeking to improve the quality, effectiveness and efficiency of the provision of the Services;
using adequate numbers of appropriately qualified Personnel in accordance with this Deed;
in accordance with all applicable Laws;
in accordance with AEC policies and specific requirements set out in the Work Order;
in accordance with any directions in relation to the Services given by the AEC from time to time;
so as to meet the Milestones and other project plan requirements, and where no Milestones or project plan requirements are specified, promptly and without delay;
in conformance with the Web Content Accessibility Guidelines 2.2 (available at ) at Level AA as a minimum where the Services involve delivery of content through a web browser;
in accordance with the same standards and obligations that are imposed on Commonwealth Personnel under the WHS Laws;
so as to keep accurate and auditable records relating to the performance of the Services; and
otherwise in accordance with the provisions of the Agreement.
Service Provider warranties
The Service Provider represents and warrants that:
it has the right to enter into this Deed and the Agreement;
it has all rights, title, licences, interests and property necessary to lawfully perform the Services;
it and its subcontractors and Personnel, including its Specified Personnel, have the necessary experience, skill, knowledge, expertise and competence to perform the Services and (where appropriate) will hold such licences, permits or registrations as are required by Law to perform the Services, and are fit and proper people;
the Services will be fit for the purpose as set out in the Statement of Requirements and Item 6 of the Work Order;
it understands its obligations under the WHS Laws, and warrants that it will comply with all of these obligations for the duration of the Deed Period;
it has and will continue to have and to use, the skills, qualifications, experience and necessary resources to provide the Services in an efficient manner with a high degree of quality and responsiveness to a standard that complies with this Deed;
the Services will be complete and accurate;
any Materials (including Third Party Material) that the Service Provider incorporates in the Agreement Material are new and free from defects in design, performance and workmanship;
it will not, nor will it suffer or permit any third party under its direction or control to negligently introduce into the AEC's ICT Systems or any Deliverables any Harmful Code;
U)		the provision of the Services will not result in the introduction of any Harmful Code into the AEC's ICT Systems;
all work performed under the Agreement will be carried out and completed in a proper and workmanlike manner and in the most cost-effective manner, and using materials suitable for the purpose;
all insurance policies required to be held by the Service Provider under this Deed and the Agreement:
will remain in effect as provided for in this Deed and the Agreement; and
will not be reduced in scope by the Service Provider without the AEC's written consent; and
(m)	it understands its obligations under the Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth), and warrants that it will comply with all of these obligations for the duration of the Deed Period.
Access to AEC's premises
The AEC must cooperate with the Service Provider by providing access to its premises and facilities as reasonably necessary to enable the Service Provider to provide the Services.
Conduct at AEC's premises
Without limiting clause 31, the Service Provider must, if using or accessing the AEC's premises or facilities, comply with all reasonable directions and procedures relating to occupational health and safety and security in operation at those premises or facilities, whether specifically drawn to the attention of the Service Provider or as might reasonably be inferred from the circumstances.
Subcontracting
The Service Provider must:
not subcontract any aspect of the provision of the Services other than to those entities set out in Item 6 of the Work Order without the prior written approval of the AEC, which will not be unreasonably withheld;
not, in any event, enter into a subcontract under an Agreement with a subcontractor named by the Workplace Gender Equality Agency as not complying with the WGE Act; and
ensure that any subcontractor approved under the Agreement complies with all applicable Laws and:
clause 16.4 (Engagement of Illegal Workers);
clause 17 (Use and protection of AEC Material and ICT Systems);

clause 30 (Political neutrality, Lobbying Code of Conduct and Conflict of Interest);
clause 31 (Security);
clause 33 (Audit and access); and
clause 38 (Insurance).
The Service Provider is fully responsible for the performance of the Services even if the Service Provider subcontracts any aspect of the provision of the Services.
In relation to the Services, the Service Provider:

agrees that the AEC may disclose publicly the names of any of the Service Provider's subcontractors; and
must ensure that any subcontractor agrees that the AEC may disclose the subcontractor's name publicly.
Co-operation with Personnel and contractors
The Service Provider must in the performance of the Services:
fully co-operate with the AEC's Personnel and other contractors; and
use its best efforts to coordinate its activities so as to support and facilitate, in the AEC's best interests, the timely and efficient completion of all work and other activities in relation to the Services to be performed for the AEC by any person.

The Service Provider must meet or exceed the Service Levels.

Where this Agreement has ended, any Fee Credits owed by the Service Provider in accordance with the Service Level Agreement will be a debt immediately due and payable by the Service Provider to the AEC.
Acknowledgment
The Service Provider acknowledges that:
the Fee Credits applied under this clause 15 and the Service Level Agreement are not a penalty, but represent a genuine pre-estimate of the expenses and loss which will be incurred by the AEC if the Service Provider does not achieve a Service Level; and
{b)		giving AEC the Fee Credits will be without prejudice to any other rights or remedies that AEC may have against the Service Provider under, or arising from,
this Deed or any Agreement as a result of the Service Provider's failure to meet the Service Levels.
GST treatment of Fee Credits
The Fee Credits are inclusive of GST and, if required under the GST Act, the Service Provider must issue an Adjustment Note in respect of any Fee Credits to the AEC.
Service Level Termination Triggers
In addition to the Fee Credits, if a Service Level Termination Trigger event occurs for reasons solely attributable to the Service Provider, then the AEC may terminate this Deed under clause 8.2(a)(iii) or any Agreement under clause 24.2(a)(iii).
Personnel
Use of Specified Personnel
The Service Provider must:
ensure that the Specified Personnel are of good fame and character, are properly qualified for the tasks they are to perform, and will act, in all the circumstances, in a fit and proper manner while carrying out the work or performing duties under this Deed;
provide the Services or any part of the Services to which their particular expertise relates, with the active involvement of, and using the expertise of, the Specified Personnel; and
ensure that each of the Specified Personnel is aware of and complies with the Service Provider's obligations in providing the Services.
If the Specified Personnel are not available
Where one or more of the Specified Personnel is or will become unable or unwilling to be involved in providing the Services, the Service Provider must notify the AEC immediately.
The Service Provider must:
if requested by the AEC, provide a replacement person of suitable ability and qualifications at no additional charge and at the earliest opportunity; and
obtain the AEC's written consent prior to appointing any such replacement person. The AEC's consent will not be unreasonably withheld.
AEC may request replacement of Personnel
To the extent they are involved in the provision of the Services, the AEC may at any time request the Service Provider to remove any of the Specified Personnel or any of the Service Provider's Personnel.
On receipt of a request under clause 16.3(a), the Service Provider must promptly arrange for the removal of such Personnel and their replacement in accordance with the process outlined in clause 16.2.
Engagement of Illegal Workers prohibited
The Service Provider must ensure that each person engaged by the Service Provider would not, in doing the work for which they are engaged, be an Illegal Worker.
The Service Provider must make compliance by any subcontractors with the provisions of this clause a condition of any subcontract.
The Service Provider must remove, or cause to be removed, any Illegal Worker from any involvement in the provision of the Services.
For the avoidance of doubt, compliance with the Service Provider's obligations under this clause will not constitute a Force Majeure Event, or give rise to an entitlement to claim any delay or otherwise excuse the Service Provider from compliance with its obligations under this Deed or any Agreement.
When requested in writing to do, the Service Provider must provide evidence within 14 days that it has taken all reasonable steps to ensure that it has complied, and is complying with, its obligations under this clause 16.4.
Use and protection of AEC Material and ICT Systems
Use of AEC Material
The AEC will provide to the Service Provider the AEC Material and the Service Provider must ensure that the AEC Material is used strictly in accordance with any conditions or restrictions specified in Item 12 of the Work Order and any direction by the AEC.
Protection against Harmful Code
The Service Provider must take all reasonable steps to ensure that neither it nor any of its Personnel, subcontractors or any other third party under its direction or control introduces any Harmful Code into the AEC's ICT Systems or any Agreement Material.
If any Harmful Code is introduced into the AEC's ICT Systems or any Agreement Material, the Service Provider must:
use all reasonable efforts to promptly report that introduction to the AEC; and
where that Harmful Code is introduced as a result of a breach of clause 17.2(a), it must:
take all necessary action to eliminate the Harmful Code; and
promptly, at its own cost, repair any harm or destruction caused by that Harmful Code.
Protection of Sensitive Election Material
This clause applies where the Services involve the movement or handling of Sensitive Election Material.
The security, integrity and accountability of Sensitive Election Material must be preserved at all times, including during transit and storage.
The Service Provider must implement and maintain the highest levels of security for Sensitive Election Material at all times and, subject to clause 17.3(d), Sensitive Election Material must never be left unattended.
In the event the Service Provider or its subcontractor stores any Sensitive Election Material, that Sensitive Election Material must be stored in secure premises, which have the following (or equivalent) safety and security measures in place:
appropriate fire detection and suppression controls to protect Sensitive Election Material;
recorded CCTV with line of sight to all Sensitive Election Material;
back to base security alarm system;
controlled access to the secure premises, using, at a minimum, an access register; and
the premises must be lockable and the Sensitive Election Material stored separately to any other material.
In the event that it is unclear to the Service Provider if the Material the subject of the Services is Sensitive Election Material or not (without opening the contents of the package), the Service Provider must treat the Material as if it contained Sensitive Election Material.
The AEC may, at its sole discretion, direct AEC Personnel to conduct an on-site inspection of the Service Provider's (or its subcontractors') premises to ensure compliance with these minimum security standards. The AEC may conduct such an inspection at any time prior to the commencement of the Services and at any other time deemed necessary by the AEC.
The Service Provider must immediately notify the AEC of any identified security issues (including any Security Incident) and remedy those issues to the satisfaction of the AEC, at the Service Provider's cost and in accordance with the applicable Service Level(s).
Intellectual Property Rights
Pre-existing Material of the Service Provider
This clause does not affect the ownership of the Intellectual Property Rights in any Pre-existing Material of the Service Provider.
The Service Provider grants to the AEC, a perpetual, irrevocable, world-wide, royalty free, non-exclusive licence (including the right to novate or assign the licence, and to sublicense) to use, reproduce, adapt, modify and communicate the Pre-existing Material of the Service Provider for the AEC's purposes.
Licence of AEC Material to Service Provider
To the extent that the Service Provider needs to use any of the AEC Material for the purpose of performing its obligations under an Agreement, the AEC grants to the Service
Provider, subject to any direction given by the AEC, a royalty-free, non-exclusive, non-transferable licence to use, reproduce, adapt, modify and communicate the AEC Material solely for the purpose of providing the Services.
Third Party Material
The Service Provider must provide Third Party Material necessary or appropriate to supply the Services and any Deliverables.
Before incorporating any Third Party Material into the Agreement Material the Service Provider must procure for the AEC perpetual licences enabling the AEC to use the Third Party Material:
to the extent necessary to obtain the full benefit of the Services; and
from the expiry or termination of this Agreement, to maintain the Deliverables or engage a third party to maintain the Deliverables.
If the Service Provider cannot obtain the licences as described in clause 18.1(b), the Service Provider must:
notify the AEC of the best alternative licence terms for that Third Party Material and not use that Third Party Material unless the AEC consents to those terms; and
if the AEC does not consent under clause 18.3(c)(i), notify the AEC of any comparable Third Party Material and comply with its obligations under this clause 18.3 in respect of comparable Third Party Material.
AEC ownership of Intellectual Property Rights in Agreement Material
All Intellectual Property Rights in Agreement Material vest in the AEC on creation.
The AEC may, at its discretion, make any and all Agreement Material available for use by the public under an Open Access Licence.
Licence of Agreement Material to the Service Provider
To the extent that the Service Provider needs to use any Agreement Material for the purpose of performing its obligations under an Agreement, the AEC grants to the Service Provider, subject to any direction given by the AEC, a royalty-free, non-exclusive, non-transferable licence to use, reproduce, adapt, modify and communicate such Material solely for the purpose of providing the Services.
Where the Service Provider wishes to use any Agreement Material otherwise than for the purpose of providing the Services, it may only do so:
with express written permission from the AEC; and
at the Service Provider's own cost.
Warranty
The Service Provider warrants that:
the Warranted Materials and the AEC's use of the Warranted Materials will not infringe the Intellectual Property Rights of any person;
it has the necessary rights to vest the Intellectual Property Rights and grant the licences as provided for in this clause 18; and
the licences conferred under clauses 18.1(b) and 18.3(b) above are sufficient to allow the AEC to make any and all Agreement Material available for use by the public under an Open Access Licence, should it elect, at its absolute discretion, to do so.
Remedy for breach of warranty
If someone claims, or the AEC reasonably believes that someone is likely to claim, that all or part of the Warranted Materials infringe their Intellectual Property Rights, the Service Provider must, in addition to the indemnity under clause 23 and to any other rights that the AEC may have against it, promptly, at the Service Provider's expense:
use its best efforts to secure the rights for the AEC to continue to use the affected Warranted Materials free of any claim or liability for infringement; or
replace or modify the affected Warranted Materials so that the Warranted Materials or the use of them does not infringe the Intellectual Property Rights of any other person without any degradation of the performance or quality of the affected Warranted Materials.
Delivery of Agreement Material
On the expiry or termination of this Agreement or on such earlier date as may be specified by the AEC, the Service Provider must deliver to the AEC Deed Manager all Agreement Material.
Moral Rights
Obtaining consents
To the extent permitted by applicable Laws and for the benefit of the AEC, the Service Provider must:
give, where the Service Provider is an individual;
use its best endeavours to ensure that each of the Personnel used by the Service Provider in the production or creation of Agreement Material gives; and
use its best endeavours to ensure that any holder of Moral Rights in Third Party Material included in Agreement Material gives,
in a form acceptable to the AEC, genuine consent in writing to the use of Agreement Material for the Specified Acts, even if such use would otherwise be an infringement of its or their Moral Rights.
Risk management
Responsibility for risk management
The Service Provider:
is responsible for the identification, control, mitigation and management of risks associated with the Services, this Deed and any Agreement; and
in particular, must:
identify and properly categorise in terms of severity all risks; and
comply at all times with AS/NZS 4360:2004 'Risk Management', as updated from time to time.
Subject to clause 20.1(c), where a party identifies a risk or change in an existing risk which may have a significant effect on the performance of this Deed or any Agreement, the party must promptly notify the other party in writing of the risk as well as its strategy to manage the risk.
The AEC is not required to disclose information which has been provided to it by the Commonwealth or another Agency and which is subject to express or implied restrictions on disclosure.
Risk Management Plan
Except where otherwise agreed by the AEC, the Service Provider must:
where no Risk Management Plan is included in Schedule 10 to this Deed, within 20 Business Days after the Commencement Date provide a draft Risk Management Plan; and
if there is a Risk Management Plan included in Schedule 10 to this Deed, at the request of either party review, update, amend and expand, as the
request requires, the Risk Management Plan and provide a revised draft Risk Management Plan,
to the AEC for approval.
The draft Risk Management Plan must:
specify identified risks;
identify who is better placed to manage each risk;
specify the measures to be taken to mitigate the risk by the party responsible for managing the risk; and
The AEC will notify the Service Provider in writing within 20 Business Days after receipt of the draft Risk Management Plan:
whether the draft Risk Management Plan is approved; or
specify amendments that it requires to be made to the draft Risk Management Plan.
The Service Provider must:
finalise the draft Risk Management Plan to produce the Risk Management Plan, if it is approved by the AEC; or
incorporate all amendments required by the AEC to be made to the draft Risk Management Plan unless the Service Provider can provide a reasonable explanation to the AEC for why it should not do so within five Business Days of receipt of the notification from AEC.
The parties will agree to amend this Deed to incorporate any approved Risk Management Plan in Schedule 10.
The Service Provider must keep the Risk Management Plan up to date, with any amendments subject to agreement by the AEC in accordance with this clause 20.2.
Compliance with Risk Management Plan
The Service Provider must manage risks in relation to the performance of its obligations under this Deed and any Agreement in accordance with the Risk Management Plan as amended from time to time.
The Service Provider must report to the AEC, as required, on:
the risks arising in relation to this Deed and any Agreement;
the management of those risks; and
the Service Provider's compliance with the Risk Management Plan.
Payment
Obligation to pay charges
Subject to this clause 21 and the Services meeting Agreement requirements including the Service Levels, the AEC must pay to the Service Provider the Service Charges as set out in Item 14 of the Work Order.
Subject to clauses 21.3 and 21.7, the AEC is under no obligation to pay any amount in excess of the Service Charges.
Service Provider to provide invoice
The Service Provider must provide a correctly rendered invoice to the AEC for the Service Charges, minus any applicable Fee Credits, in accordance with the requirements specified in Schedule 3 and the Service Level Agreement.
Due date for payment
The AEC will pay the Service Provider within 20 days after receipt of a correctly rendered invoice. If this period ends on a day that is not a Business Day, payment is due on the next Business Day.
Interest for late payment
This clause 21.4 only applies where:
the initial value of the Agreement (excluding any options, extensions, renewals or other mechanisms that may be executed over the life of the Agreement) is less than $1 million (GST inclusive); and
the amount of the interest payable exceeds $100.
For payments made by the AEC more than 20 days after the amount became due and payable, the AEC must pay interest accrued on the payment.
Interest payable under this clause 21.4 will be simple interest on the unpaid amount calculated in respect of each day from the day after the amount was due and payable, up to and including the day that the AEC effects payment as represented by the following formula:
SI	= UA	x	GIC  x	D
where:
SI	= the simple interest amount; UA = the unpaid amount;
GIC = the general interest charge rate determined under section 8AAD of the Taxation Administration Act 1953 (Cth) on the day payment is due, expressed as a decimal rate per day; and
Note: See the ATO webpage at: https://www.ato.gov.au/tax-rates-and-codes/general-interest-charge-rates.
D	= the number of days from the day after the amount was due and payable up to and including the day that the AEC makes the payment.
Correct rendering of invoices
An invoice is correctly rendered if:
it is correctly addressed and calculated in accordance with the Agreement;
it relates only to supplies that have been delivered to the AEC in accordance with the Agreement; and
it is a valid tax invoice in accordance with the GST Act.
Incorrect invoices, under/over payment
If an invoice is found to have been rendered incorrectly after payment, any underpayment or overpayment will be recoverable by or from the Service Provider, as the case may be, and, without limiting recourse to other available means, may be offset against any amount subsequently due by the AEC to the Service Provider under the Agreement.
Expenses
The Service Provider may only claim expenses as set out in Schedule 3 and the Work Order.
Taxes
The Service Provider must pay:
all stamp duty (including penalties and interest) assessed or payable in respect of this Deed and the Agreement and the undertaking of the Services; and
subject to clause 22, all taxes, duties and government charges imposed or levied in Australia or overseas in connection with the performance of this Deed and the Agreement.
GST
Interpretation
In this clause 22, a word or expression defined in the GST Act has the meaning given to it in that Act.
GST gross up
If a party (Supplier) makes a supply under or in connection with the Agreement in respect of which GST is payable, the recipient of the supply (Recipient) must pay to the Supplier an additional amount equal to the GST payable on the supply (GST Amount).
Reimbursements
If a party must reimburse or indemnify another party for a Loss, the amount to be reimbursed or indemnified is first reduced by any input tax credit the other party is entitled to for the Loss and then increased in accordance with clause 22.2.
Exclusion of GST from calculations
If a payment is calculated by reference to or as a specified percentage of another amount or revenue stream, that payment will be calculated by reference to or as a specified percentage of the amount or revenue stream exclusive of GST.
Adjustments
If the GST payable by a Supplier on any supply made under or in connection with the Agreement varies from the GST Amount paid or payable by the Recipient under clause 22.2 such that a further amount of GST is payable in relation to the supply or a refund or credit of GST is obtained in relation to the supply, then the Supplier will provide a corresponding refund or credit to, or will be entitled to receive the amount of that variation from, the Recipient.
Any payment, credit or refund under this clause is deemed to be a payment, credit or refund of the GST Amount payable under clause 22.2.
If an adjustment event occurs in relation to a supply, the Supplier must issue an Adjustment Note to the Recipient in relation to that supply within 14 days after becoming aware of the adjustment.
Tax invoice
A party need not make a payment for a taxable supply made under or in connection with the Agreement until it receives a tax invoice for the supply to which the payment relates.
Indemnity
Indemnity
The Service Provider indemnifies at all times, and holds harmless the AEC (including its Personnel) against, all:
Losses incurred by the AEC in procuring goods and services similar to the Services from another supplier if this Deed or any Agreement is terminated by the AEC for default of the Service Provider;
Losses suffered or incurred by the AEC, including as the result of any claim made in relation to:
loss of or damage to third party property; 
a breach of the privacy of an individual to which clause 27.4 applies; or
the injury, illness or death of a third party;
loss of or damage to the AEC's property; or
Losses suffered or incurred by the AEC in dealing with any claim against the AEC, including legal costs and expenses on a solicitor/ own client basis and includes for the avoidance of doubt, the costs of time spent, resources used, or disbursements paid by the AEC,
arising from:
any act or omission by the Service Provider (including by any of its Personnel or subcontractors) in connection with this Deed or the Agreement;
any breach by the Service Provider (including by any of its Personnel or subcontractors) of its obligations or warranties under this Deed or the Agreement;
any use or disclosure by the Service Provider (including by its Personnel or subcontractors) of Personal Information or Confidential Information (or both, as the case may be) held or controlled in connection with this Agreement; or
the use by the AEC of Agreement Material, including the Intellectual Property Rights and Moral Rights comprised in Agreement Material.
Proportional reduction of liability
The liability of a party (Party A) for any Losses incurred by another party (Party 8) will be reduced proportionately to the extent that any negligent act or omission of Party B (or of its subcontractors or Personnel) contributed to those Losses, regardless of whether legal proceedings are brought by Party A for negligence or breach of contract.
AEC's right to be indemnified is additional to other rights
The AEC's right to be indemnified under clause 23.1 is in addition to, and not exclusive of, any other right, power, or remedy provided by Law, but the AEC is not entitled to be compensated in excess of the amount of the relevant Loss.
Termination of Agreement
Termination and reduction for convenience
The AEC may, at any time, by notice, terminate the Agreement or reduce the scope of the Services, including for, or as a result of, a machinery of government change or the
establishment of a whole of Australian Government panel or coordinated process for the Services.
Termination for default
Without limiting any other rights or remedies the AEC may have against the Service Provider arising out of or in connection with the Agreement, the AEC may terminate the Agreement or reduce the scope of the Services effective immediately by giving notice to the Service Provider if:
the Service Provider breaches a material provision of the Agreement where that breach is not capable of remedy;
the Service Provider breaches any provision of the Agreement and fails to remedy the breach within 14 days after receiving notice requiring it to do so;
a Service Level Termination Trigger event occurs for reasons solely attributable to the Service Provider;
in the opinion of the AEC, a Conflict of Interest exists which would prevent the Service Provider from properly performing its obligations under the Agreement; or
subject to the AEC complying with any requirements in the Corporations Act 2001 (Cth), an event specified in clause 24.2(c) happens to the Service Provider.
Without limitation, for the purposes of clause 24.2 (a)(i), each of the following constitutes a breach of a material provision:
a breach of warranty under clause 12.2 (Service Provider warranties);
a failure to comply with clause 12.5(a)(ii) (workplace gender equality and subcontracting);
a failure to comply with clause 16 (Personnel);
a failure to comply with clause 18 (Intellectual Property Rights);
a failure to comply with clause 26 (Confidentiality and privacy);
a failure to comply with clause 27 (Protection of Personal Information);
a failure to comply with clause 30 (Political neutrality, Lobbying Code of Conduct and Conflict of Interest);
a failure to comply with clause 31 (Security);
a failure to comply with clause 34 (Workplace legislation);
any material failure by the Service Provider to:
implement the Indigenous Participation Plan in accordance with clause 35; or
comply with a direction issued by the AEC Deed Manager under clause 35.3(e);
a failure to comply with clause 37B (Supplier Code of Conduct);
a failure to comply with clause 37C (Notification of Significant Events); or
a failure to comply with clause 38 (Insurance).
The Service Provider must notify the AEC immediately if:
the Service Provider is a corporation and there is any change in the direct or indirect beneficial ownership or control of the Service Provider;
the Service Provider disposes of the whole or any part of its assets, operations or business other than in the ordinary course of business;
the Service Provider ceases to carry on business;
the Service Provider ceases to be able to pay its debts as they become due;
the Service Provider being a company enters into liquidation or has a controller or managing controller or liquidator or administrator appointed;
the Service Provider being a natural person is declared bankrupt or assigns his or her estate for the benefit of creditors; or
where the Service Provider is a partnership, any step is taken to dissolve that partnership.
In this clause, controller, managing controller and administrator have the same meanings as in the Corporations Act.
Consequences of termination generally
On receipt of a notice of termination or reduction the Service Provider must:
stop work as specified in the notice;
take all available steps to minimise Loss resulting from that termination and to protect AEC Material and Agreement Material;
deal with Agreement Material in accordance with clause 18.8; and
repay on demand any advance payments made by the AEC for Services not provided by the effective termination date.
Consequences of termination for convenience
If the Agreement is terminated under clause 24.1, the AEC is liable only for:
payments under clause 21 for Services rendered in accordance with the Agreement before the effective date of termination, minus any applicable Fee Credits; and
reasonable costs actually incurred by the Service Provider and directly attributable to the termination.
The AEC is not liable to pay compensation under clause 24.4(a)(ii) for an amount which would, in addition to any amounts paid or due, or becoming due, to the Service Provider under the Agreement, exceed the total Fees payable under the Agreement.
The Service Provider is not entitled to compensation for loss of prospective profits or loss of opportunity.
Consequences of scope reduction
If the scope of the Services is reduced:
the AEC's liability to pay the Fees or to provide AEC Material abates in accordance with the reduction in the Services; and
the Service Provider must comply with the AEC's directions about the Services remaining in scope and take all available steps to minimise Loss resulting from the reduction in scope.
Deemed termination
If a purported termination by the AEC under clause 24.1 is determined by a competent authority to be invalid for any reason, then that termination will be deemed to be a termination under clause 24.2, which has effect from the date of the notice of termination referred to in clause 24.1.
Termination does not affect accrued rights
Termination of the Agreement does not affect any accrued rights or remedies of a party.
Survival
The following clauses survive the expiry or termination of the Agreement:
clause 12.5 (Subcontracting);
clause 15.2(b) (Fee Credits);
clause 18 (Intellectual Property Rights);
clause 19 (Moral Rights);
clause 21.6 (Incorrect invoices, under/over payment);
clause 22 (GST);
clause 23 (Indemnity);
clause 24.3 (Consequences of termination generally);
clause 24.4 (Consequences of termination for convenience);
clause 24.7 (Termination does not affect accrued rights); and
Part 3 - General requirements
Confidentiality and privacy
Prohibition on disclosure
Subject to clause 26.4:
the Service Provider must not, without the prior written consent of the AEC, disclose any AEC Confidential Information to a third party; and
the AEC must not, without the prior written consent of the Service Provider, disclose any Service Provider Confidential Information to a third party.
Conditions of approval
In giving written consent to use or disclose AEC Confidential Information, the AEC may impose reasonable conditions as it thinks fit.
The Service Provider must comply with any term or condition imposed by the AEC under this clause 26.2.
Advisers and third parties
The AEC may at any time require the Service Provider to arrange for:
its Advisers;
its Personnel engaged in the performance of the Services (including Specified Personnel); or
any other third party, to whom AEC Confidential Information may be disclosed pursuant to clauses 26.4(a), (b) or (c),
to give a written undertaking relating to the use and non-disclosure of the AEC's Confidential Information substantially in the form set out in Schedule 9.
Exceptions to obligations
The obligations on each party under clause 26.1 will not be taken to have been breached to the extent that Confidential Information of the other party:
is disclosed by a party to its Advisers or employees solely in order to comply with obligations, or to exercise rights, under this Deed;
is disclosed to a party's internal management Personnel, solely to enable effective management or auditing of activities related to this Deed;
is disclosed to a subcontractor or related entity for the purposes of performing its obligations under this Deed;
is disclosed by the AEC to the responsible Minister;
(e.)
is disclosed by the AEC, in response to a request by a House or a Committee of the Parliament of the Commonwealth;
is shared by the AEC within the Commonwealth, or with another Agency, where this serves the Commonwealth's legitimate interests, such as for policy development, research, compliance, regulatory and other non-commercial purposes;
is disclosed by the AEC to the Auditor General, Ombudsman, Privacy Commissioner, Information Commissioner or Freedom of Information Commissioner or their delegates;
is required by Law, the rules of a stock exchange or this Deed to be disclosed; or
is in the public domain otherwise than due to a breach of this Deed.
Obligation on disclosure
Where a party discloses Confidential Information of the other party to another person:
(a)  pursuant to clauses 26.4(a), (b), (c) or (f), the disclosing party must:
notify the receiving person that the information is Confidential Information; and
not provide the information unless the receiving person agrees to keep the information confidential, including in the case of AEC Confidential Information, the receiving person giving the AEC a legally binding undertaking to that effect substantially in the form set out in Schedule 9; or
(b)	pursuant to clauses 26.4(d) or (e), the disclosing party must notify the receiving party that the information is Confidential Information of the other party.
Additional confidential information
The parties may agree in writing after the Commencement Date that certain additional information is to constitute Confidential Information for the purposes of this Deed (Additional Confidential Information).
This Additional Confidential Information is incorporated into, and becomes part of the Confidential Information under this Deed, on the date both parties agree in writing under clause 26.6(a).
Period of confidentiality
The obligations under this clause 26 continue, notwithstanding the expiry or termination of this Deed:
in relation to an item of information described in Schedule 5 or the Work Order, for the period set out in Schedule 5 or the Work Order in respect of that item; and
in relation to any Additional Confidential Information for the purposes of this Deed, for the period agreed by the parties in writing in respect of that information.
No reduction in privacy obligations
Nothing in this Deed derogates from any obligation which either party may have under the Privacy Act as amended from time to time, in relation to the protection of Personal
Information or information that is protected by the Census and Statistics Act 1905 (Cth), or any other Law requiring secrecy or confidentiality in dealing with information.
Return of information
Subject to clause 26.9(b), at the AEC's request or on the expiry or termination of this Deed, the Service Provider must promptly return all of the AEC's physical and written records containing AEC Confidential Information, and all documentation relating to that AEC Confidential Information (including copies), to the AEC in a form reasonably requested by the AEC.
If requested by the AEC, the Service Provider must destroy such items in the manner specified by the AEC and promptly certify to the AEC in writing that it has done so.
Confidential provisions
Notwithstanding any other provision of this Deed, the AEC may disclose the provisions of this Deed.
Protection of Personal Information
Application of this clause
This clause 27 applies only where the Service Provider deals with Personal Information when, and for the purposes of, providing Services under this Deed.
Obligations
The Service Provider acknowledges that it is a 'contracted service provider' within the meaning of section 6 of the Privacy Act, and must in respect of the provision of Services under this Deed:
use or disclose Personal Information obtained during the course of providing Services under this Deed, only for the purposes of this Deed;
not to do any act or engage in any practice that would breach an Australian Privacy Principle (APP), which if done or engaged in by an Agency, would be a breach of that APP;
carry out and discharge the obligations contained in the APPs as if it were an Agency under the Privacy Act;
not take or allow Personal Information to be taken outside of Australia in a manner inconsistent with clause 31.7(c);
if requested by the AEC to do so, arrange for all Personnel of the Service Provider to whom Personal Information is to be disclosed to give a written undertaking substantially in the form set out in Schedule 9;
notify individuals whose Personal Information the Service Provider holds, that complaints about acts or practices of the Service Provider may be investigated by the Privacy Commissioner, Information Commissioner and / or Freedom of Information Commissioner who has power to award compensation against the Service Provider in appropriate circumstances;
immediately notify the AEC if the Service Provider becomes aware of a breach or possible breach of any of the obligations contained, or referred to, in this clause 27, whether by the Service Provider or any subcontractor;
comply with any directions, guidelines, determinations or recommendations of the Privacy Commissioner, Information Commissioner and / or Freedom of Information Commissioner, to the extent that they are not inconsistent with the requirements of this clause 27; and
ensure that any Personnel of the Service Provider who is required to deal with Personal Information for the purposes of this Deed is made aware of the obligations of the Service Provider set out in this clause 27.
Without derogating from any other obligations of the Service Provider under Law or otherwise, the Service Provider must assist the AEC to enable any person, on request, to ascertain in respect of that person's Personal Information:
whether the Service Provider has possession or control of any records that contain such Personal Information;
the nature of the information;
the main purposes for which that person's Personal Information is used by the Service Provider; and
the steps that the person should take if the person wishes to obtain access to the Personal Information.
Subcontracts
The Service Provider must ensure that any subcontract entered into for the purpose of fulfilling its obligations under this Deed contains provisions to ensure that the subcontractor has the same awareness and obligations as the Service Provider has under this clause 27, including this requirement in relation to subcontracts.
Indemnity
Where liability has not been apportioned by the Privacy Commissioner, the Service Provider indemnifies the AEC in respect of any Loss suffered or incurred by the AEC which arises directly or indirectly from a breach of any of the obligations of the Service Provider under this clause 27, or a subcontractor under the subcontract provisions referred to in clause 27.2(a).
Access to documents
Application of clause
This clause 28 only applies if the Services involve the provision of services by the Service Provider (but not goods or financial assistance grants) for or on behalf of the AEC to a person who is not the Commonwealth or an Agency.
Access to security classified information
Where the Service Provider and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) may have access to security classified information of the AEC, the Service Provider and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) involved in providing the Services must complete, to the AEC's satisfaction, the Security Awareness Training Program, prior to accessing security classified information.
Provision of documents
Where the AEC has received a request for access to a document created by, or in the possession of, the Service Provider or any subcontractor that relates to the performance of this Deed (and not to the entry into this Deed), the AEC may at any time by written notice require the Service Provider to provide the document to the AEC and the Service Provider must, at no additional cost to the AEC, promptly comply with the notice.
The Service Provider must include in any subcontract relating to the performance of this Deed provisions that will enable the Service Provider to comply with its obligations under this clause 28.
In this clause, document has the same meaning as it has in the Freedom of Information Act 1982 (Cth).
Public interest disclosure
Application
The Service Provider acknowledges that:
the AEC is an agency that is subject to the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013
(Cth) (the PID Act);
this Deed may be a 'Commonwealth contract', the Service Provider and its subcontractors may be a 'contracted Contractor' and any officers and employees of the Service Provider or its subcontractors may be a 'public official', within the meaning of the PID Act; and
conduct engaged in by the Service Provider or any subcontractor in relation to entering into, or giving effect to this Deed, may be 'disclosable conduct' under the PID Act.
Compliance
The parties must promptly comply with their obligations under the PID Act and the AEC's public interest disclosure policies, at no additional cost to one another, and the Service Provider must ensure that all of its subcontractors also comply with this clause 29 and the PID Act.
Political neutrality, Lobbying Code of Conduct and Conflict of Interest
Political neutrality of the AEC
The Service Provider must:
respect the strict political neutrality of the AEC;
not jeopardise the AEC's political neutrality by any act or omission; and
not associate the AEC in any way with any political activity that they undertake.
Political neutrality of Service Provider Personnel
Where the Service Provider supplies Service Provider Personnel to provide any of the Services, the AEC in its absolute discretion may:
require the Service Provider to ensure that those Service Provider Personnel sign a declaration of political neutrality substantially in the form set out in Schedule 9 to ensure that such Personnel are aware of the requirement in clause 30.1; and
request the removal of such Service Provider Personnel who do not sign that declaration of political neutrality from provision of the Services, in accordance with this Deed.
Lobbying Code of Conduct
The Service Provider:
acknowledges that a person engaged as a contractor by the AEC will be a 'government representative' for the purposes of the Lobbying Code of Conduct; and
must, and must ensure that its subcontractors, comply with:
the Lobbying Code of Conduct; and
the Australian Public Service Commission (APSC) Circular 2008/4, Requirements relating to the Lobbying Code of Conduct and post separation contact with Government,
where their activities fall within the scope of the Lobbying Code of Conduct.
Conflict of Interest warranty
The Service Provider warrants that, to the best of its knowledge after making diligent inquiry, at the date of signing this Deed and at the formation of each Agreement, no Conflict of Interest exists or is likely to arise in the performance of its obligations under this Deed.
Notification of a Conflict of Interest
If, during the performance of the Services a Conflict of Interest arises, or appears likely to arise, the Service Provider must:
notify the AEC immediately in writing;
make full disclosure of all relevant information relating to the Conflict of Interest and setting out the steps the Service Provider proposes to take to resolve or otherwise deal with the Conflict of Interest; and
take such steps as the AEC requires to resolve or otherwise deal with the Conflict of Interest.
Security
Compliance with Australian Government and AEC requirements
The Service Provider must, and must ensure that its subcontractors and Personnel comply with:
all relevant security and other requirements specified in the Protective Security Policy Framework; and
any other security procedures or requirements notified, in writing, by the AEC to the Service Provider.
The Service Provider must comply with such a security procedure or requirement, from the date specified in the notice, or if none is specified, within five Business Days of receipt of the notice.
31A	Cyber security
Without limiting the requirements in a Statement of Requirements, the Service Provider must take reasonable and prudent steps consistent with industry best practice to reduce the risk of Cyber Attack on Service Provider IT Systems.
At the AEC's written request, the Service Provider must promptly provide details of the Service Provider's security measures in place to reduce the risk of Cyber Attack on Service Provider’s IT Systems.
Security clearance and Police Checks
The AEC may, from time to time, notify the Service Provider of the level of security or access clearance applicable to the Service Provider's Personnel, and the date from which, or the period during which, that clearance will be effective and the Service Provider must comply with and ensure its Personnel act in accordance with that notice.
The Service Provider must, if requested by the AEC, ensure that any Personnel (including Specified Personnel) engaged by or on behalf of the Service Provider meets all necessary security assessments and standards required by the AEC including:
an identity check;
a national police clearance check or equivalent;
signing any forms notified to the Service Provider by the AEC from time to time;
holding Commonwealth security clearances to the level requested by the AEC in accordance with the Protective Security Policy Framework, or as otherwise required by the AEC;
attending and completing an AEC security awareness course prior to or as soon as possible after the Commencement Date at the Service Provider's cost; and
complying with any other security awareness requirements reasonably requested by the AEC.
The Service Provider must prevent Personnel (including Specified Personnel) whose security clearances are revoked, lapse or who no longer require access, from:
having access to AEC premises and AEC Data; and
providing the Services.
The Service Provider must report to the AEC any incidental or accidental contact with security classified information by Personnel (including Specified Personnel) as soon as possible but not less than 5 Business Days from the incidental or accidental contact with the security classified information.
The Service Provider is responsible for all costs and expenses associated with obtaining security clearances.
Storage of security classified information
This clause 31.2(a) applies where the Service Provider will handle or store security classified information of the AEC outside the AEC's premises and/or systems.
The Service Provider must ensure that the premises and facilities used to handle or store security classified information of the AEC meet the appropriate security level as determined by the AEC.
The Service Provider must have the appropriate systems able to meet designated information security standards for the electronic processing, storage, transmission and disposal of official information.
The Service Provider bears all the costs and expenses associated with ensuring that the storage of security classified information meets the appropriate security level as determined by the AEC.
Security Incidents
The Service Provider must report to the AEC any actual or suspected Security Incident as soon as possible but no less than five Business Days from the actual or suspected Security Incident.
The Service Provider must report any incidents or breaches of information and / or communications technology security, not involving AEC Material to the Australian Cyber Security Centre as soon as possible but not less than five Business Days from the incident or breach.
Highest level of security classified information
The Service Provider may have access to security classified information up to the level specified in Item 9 of the Deed Details. If requested by the AEC, the Service Provider must provide evidence that the Specified Personnel have the requisite security clearances to access this information within the timeframe specified in the request.
Essential protective security requirements
The Service Provider must comply with the essential protective security requirements specified in Item 10 of the Deed Details.
Restrictions on use of AEC Data
AEC Data is and remains the property of the AEC.
The Service Provider must not, and must ensure that its subcontractors and Personnel do not:
use AEC Data for any purpose other than directly in relation to the provision of the Services to the AEC;
purport to sell, let for hire, assign rights in or otherwise dispose of any AEC Data;
make any AEC Data available to any third party other than a subcontractor and then only to the extent necessary to enable the subcontractor to perform its part of this Deed or the applicable Work Order; 
commercially or otherwise exploit AEC Data; or
alter AEC Data in any way, other than for the purposes of performing the Services.
The Service Provider must not, and must ensure that its subcontractors and Personnel do not:
remove AEC Data or allow AEC Data to be removed from the AEC's premises; or
take AEC Data transfer, transmit or disclose AEC Data or allow AEC Data to be taken, transferred, transmitted, accessed or disclosed outside of Australia,
without the AEC's prior written consent. 
(d)	The prohibition in clause 31.7(c) applies in respect of taking, transferring, transmitting, accessing or otherwise disclosing AEC Data outside of Australia by the Service Provider:
(i)	within the Contractor's own organisation; and 
(ii)	to any third party, including to any Subcontractor
Where such consent is granted, offshore storage of AEC Data (including in any virtual environment such as the cloud) must be in a manner consistent with all applicable Commonwealth policies including the Information Security Manual and the Protective Security Policy Framework.
Security training
The Service Provider must ensure that, before any of the Service Provider Personnel or subcontractors have access to or are permitted to use AEC Data in any way, they have received any training required by the AEC in relation to the handling and use of that AEC Data.
The Service Provider must bear the cost of any training under clause 31.8(a).
Safeguarding AEC Data
The Service Provider must and must ensure that its Personnel establish and maintain all appropriate physical, technical and organisational safeguards to protect AEC Data in its possession or control against the destruction, unauthorised disclosure, access, loss, damage, processing, erasure, transfer, use, modification, misuse or alteration that:
are no less rigorous than those notified by the AEC from time to time and will be no less rigorous than the safeguards that can be reasonably expected according to industry best practice; and
comply with all Law and any procedures or requirements specified by the AEC from time to time.  
The Service Provider agrees that the AEC and its internal and external auditors, inspectors and such other representatives as the AEC may designate from time to time may, at any time, with reasonable notice, conduct a security audit of the Service Provider's and its subcontractors’ compliance with this clause 31, including the right to undertake a security risk assessment, threat risk assessment or privacy impact assessment.
The Service Provider must: 
	(i)	maintain daily backups of AEC Data in accordance with industry best practice and in such a manner that allows a retrieval of AEC Data to be no older than 24 hours before any issue first affected such AEC Data; and
	(ii)	make reasonable efforts to identify and advise the AEC in writing of, as soon as reasonably practicable, any issues affecting AEC Data which may give rise to a claim for indemnity under clause 23 of this Deed.    
Nothing in this clause 31 is intended to limit the Service Provider's obligations contemplated by this Deed.
Delete, destroy or return of AEC Data
Immediately upon termination or expiry of this Deed, the Service Provider must, and must ensure its Subcontractors:
deliver to the AEC all AEC Data and storage media; and
erase or destroy all electronic records containing AEC Data, which are in the possession, power or control of the Service Provider or of any person to whom the Service Provider has given access.
Upon the AEC’s written request, the Service Provider must certify that deletion or destruction of AEC Data has taken place.
The Service Provider must and must ensure that its subcontractors, perform such deletion or destruction in an appropriate and auditable manner that ensures that AEC Data cannot be re-created, accessed or read.
31.11	Subcontracts
(a)    The Service Provider must ensure that any subcontract entered into for the purpose of fulfilling its obligations under this Deed imposes on the subcontractor the same obligations as the Service Provider has under this clause 31, including the requirement in relation to subcontracts.
Books and records
Service Provider to keep books and records
The Service Provider must:
keep and require its subcontractors to keep comprehensive, accurate and adequate books and records in accordance with Australian Accounting Standards, in sufficient detail to enable the amounts payable by the AEC under this Deed to be determined; and
retain and require its subcontractors to retain all books and records relating to the Services for a period of seven years after the expiry or termination of this Deed or such longer period as may be required by Law.
Costs
The Service Provider must bear its own costs of complying with this clause 32.
Audit and access
Right to conduct audits
The AEC or a representative may conduct audits relevant to the performance of the Service Provider's obligations under this Deed. Audits may be conducted of:
the Service Provider's premises, operational practices and procedures as they relate to this Deed, including security procedures;
the accuracy of the Service Provider's invoices and reports in relation to the provision of the Services under this Deed;
the Service Provider's compliance with its confidentiality, privacy and security obligations under this Deed;
Material (including books and records) in the possession of the Service Provider relevant to the Services or this Deed; and
any other matters determined by the AEC to be relevant to the Services or this Deed.
Access by the AEC
The AEC may, at reasonable times and on giving reasonable notice to the Service Provider:
access the premises of the Service Provider to the extent relevant to the performance of this Deed;
require the provision by the Service Provider and its Personnel or subcontractors of records and information in a data format and storage medium accessible by the AEC by use of the AEC's existing computer hardware and software;
inspect and copy any Material, however stored, in the custody or under the control of the Service Provider and its Personnel or subcontractors; and
require assistance in respect of any inquiry into or concerning the Services or this Deed. For these purposes an inquiry includes any administrative or statutory review, audit or inquiry (whether within or external to the AEC), any request for information directed to the AEC, and any inquiry conducted by Parliament or any Parliamentary committee.
The Service Provider must promptly provide access to its computer hardware and software to the extent necessary for the AEC to exercise its rights under this clause 33, and provide the AEC with any reasonable assistance requested by the AEC to use that hardware and software.
Conduct of audit and access
The AEC must use reasonable endeavours to ensure that:
audits performed pursuant to clause 33.1; and
the exercise of the general rights granted by clause 33.2 by the AEC,
do not unreasonably delay or disrupt (in any material respect) the Service Provider's performance of its obligations under this Deed or its business.
Costs
Unless otherwise agreed in writing, each party must bear its own costs of any reviews and/or audits.
Auditor-General, Ombudsman and Commissioners
The rights of the AEC under clause 33.2(a)(i) to 33.2(a)(iv) apply equally to the Auditor-General, Ombudsman, Information Commissioner, Privacy Commissioner and Freedom of Information Commissioner, or his or her delegate, for the purpose of performing their respective statutory functions or powers.
Service Provider to comply with requirements
The Service Provider must do all things necessary to comply with the Auditor-General's, Ombudsman's, Information Commissioner's, Privacy Commissioner's or Freedom of Information Commissioner's or his or her delegate's requirements, notified under
clause 33.2, provided such requirements are legally enforceable and within their respective statutory functions or powers.
No reduction in responsibility
The requirement for, and participation in, audits does not in any way reduce the Service Provider's responsibility to perform its obligations in accordance with this Deed.
Subcontractor requirements
The Service Provider must ensure that any subcontract entered into for the purpose of this Deed contains an equivalent clause granting the rights specified in this clause 33.
No restriction
Nothing in this Deed reduces, limits or restricts in any way any function, power, right or entitlement of the Auditor-General, Ombudsman, Information Commissioner, Privacy Commissioner or Freedom of Information Commissioner, or his or her delegate.
The rights of the AEC under this Deed are in addition to any other power, right or entitlement of the Auditor-General, Ombudsman, Information Commissioner, Privacy Commissioner or Freedom of Information Commissioner, or his or her delegate.
Survival
This clause applies for the Deed Period and for a period of seven years from the termination or expiry of this Deed.
Workplace legislation
Work health and safety
The Service Provider must in carrying out its obligations under this Deed, comply, and use reasonable endeavours to ensure that its subcontractors comply, with the provisions of all relevant Laws and requirements of any Commonwealth, State, Territory or local authority including those arising under a WHS Law in respect of occupational health and safety.
The Service Provider must, in carrying out its obligations under this Deed, comply, and use reasonable endeavours to ensure that its subcontractors comply, with any of the Commonwealth's work, health and safety policies as notified, referred to, or made available, by the Commonwealth to the Service Provider in writing.
If the Service Provider is required by a WHS Law to report to a Regulator an incident arising out of the provision of the Services:
at the same time, or as soon as is possible in the circumstances, the Service Provider must give notice of such incident, and a copy of any written notice provided to a Regulator, to the AEC; and
the Service Provider must provide to the AEC, within such time as is specified by the AEC, a report detailing the circumstances of the incident, the results of investigations into its cause, and any recommendations or strategies for prevention in the future.
The Service Provider must inform the AEC of the full details of:
any suspected contravention of a WHS Law relating to the provision of the Services, within 24 hours of becoming aware of any such suspected contravention;
any cessation or direction to cease work relating to the provision of the Services, due to unsafe work, immediately upon the Service Provider being informed of any such cessation or direction;
any workplace entry by a WHS entry permit holder, or an inspector, to any place where the Services are being performed or undertaken, within 24 hours of becoming aware of any such workplace entry; and
any proceedings against the Service Provider or its officers, or any decision or request by the Regulator given to the Service Provider or its Personnel,
under a WHS Law, within 24 hours of becoming aware of any such proceedings, decision or request.
WGE Act
This clause applies only to the extent that the Service Provider is a 'relevant employer' for the purposes of the WGE Act.
The Service Provider must comply with its obligations, if any, under the WGE Act.
If the Service Provider becomes non-compliant with the WGE Act during the Deed Period, the Service Provider must promptly notify the AEC Deed Manager of that fact.
If the Deed Period exceeds 18 months, the Service Provider must provide a current letter of compliance within 18 months from the Commencement Date and following this, annually, to the AEC Deed Manager.
Compliance with the WGE Act does not relieve the Service Provider, from its responsibility to comply with its other obligations under this Deed.

Overview
It is Commonwealth policy to stimulate Indigenous entrepreneurship and business development, providing Indigenous Australians with more opportunities to participate in the economy (see the Indigenous Procurement Policy for further information).

purchasing from Indigenous Enterprises; and
employment of Indigenous Australians, in the delivery of the Services.

Indigenous Participation Plan

Compliance and reporting

comply with the Indigenous Participation Plan;

comply with any directions issued by the AEC Deed Manager in relation to the Service Provider's implementation of the Indigenous Participation Plan; and
report on its compliance with the Indigenous Participation Plan to the AEC Deed Manager within 10 Business Days after the end of the Agreement Period (End of Term Report).
The End of Term Report must identify whether the Service Provider:
met the mandatory minimum requirements in the Indigenous Procurement Policy; and
complied with the Indigenous Participation Plan.
If the Service Provider did not comply with the Indigenous Participation Plan it must provide an explanation for any non-compliance (including partial non-compliance).
If the AEC Deed Manager considers, in its absolute discretion at any time during the Agreement Period, that it has concerns in relation to the Service Provider's:
compliance with the Indigenous Participation Plan; or
overall ability to meet the mandatory minimum requirements as set out in the Indigenous Procurement Policy,
the AEC Deed Manager may request the Service Provider to provide additional detail in relation to its implementation of and overall ability to comply with the Indigenous Participation Plan.
The Service Provider must comply with all reasonable directions issued by the AEC Deed Manager in relation to the Service Provider's implementation of the Indigenous Participation Plan.
Termination rights
Without limiting its other rights under this Deed or at Law, any material failure by the Service Provider to:
implement the Indigenous Participation Plan; or
comply with a direction issued by the AEC Deed Manager under clause 35.3(e),
will be a material breach of this Deed, and the AEC may terminate this Deed in accordance with clause 24.2.
Use of reports
Notwithstanding any other clause of this Deed, the Service Provider acknowledges and agrees that the reports it submits under clauses 35.3(a)(ii) and 35.3(a)(iv):
will be recorded in a central database that is able to be accessed by Commonwealth entities and may be made publicly available;
will not be considered to be Service Provider Confidential Information; and
may be used by Commonwealth entities for any purpose, including for evaluation of an offer to provide the Services to a Commonwealth entity.
