37A.  National Anti-Corruption Commission
The Service Provider acknowledges that in providing the Services to the AEC under this Agreement, it is a contracted service provider for the purposes of the National Anti-Corruption Commission Act 2022 (Cth) (NACC Act). The Service Provider and its officers or employees engaged in the performance of this Agreement (and any subcontractors) will be public officials under the NACC Act. 
The Service Provider must comply with any reasonable request, policy or direction issued by the AEC and otherwise cooperate with the AEC in relation to any action taken by the AEC required or authorised by the NACC Act.
37B. Supplier Code of Conduct
For the purposes of this clause, ‘Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct’ or ‘Code’ means the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct, as published on 1 July 2024, as updated from time to time.
The Service Provider must comply with, and ensure that it’s officers, employees, agents and subcontractors comply with, the Code in connection with the performance of this Deed.
The Service Provider must:
periodically monitor and assess its, and its officers’, employees’, and agents’ compliance with the Code; and
on request from the AEC, promptly provide information regarding:
the policies, frameworks, or systems it has established to monitor and assess compliance with the Code, and
the Service Provider’s compliance with clause 37B(b).
The Service Provider must immediately issue the AEC a Notice on becoming aware of any breach of clause 37B(b). The Notice must include a summary of the breach, the date that the breach occurred, and details of the personnel involved.
Where the AEC identifies a possible breach of clause 37B(b), it may issue the Service Provider a Notice, and the Supplier must, within three (3) Business Days of receiving the Notice, either:
where the Service Provider considers a breach has not occurred: advise the AEC that there has not been a breach and provide information supporting that determination; or
where the Service Provider considers that a breach has occurred: issue a Notice under clause 37B(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 37B.
Notwithstanding clause 37B(e), the AEC may notify the Service Provider in writing that it considers that the Service Provider has breached clause 37B(b), in which case the Service Provider must issue a Notice under clause 37B(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 37B.
A failure by the Service Provider to comply with its obligations under any part of this clause will be a material breach of this Deed.
Nothing in this clause or the Code limits, reduces, or derogates from the Service Provider’s other obligations under this Deed. The AEC’s rights under this clause are in addition to and do not otherwise limit any other rights the AEC may have under this Deed. The performance by the Service Provider of its obligations under this clause will be at no additional cost to the AEC.
The Service Provider agrees that the AEC or any other Commonwealth agency may take into account the Service Provider’s compliance with the Code in any future approach to market or procurement process.
37C. Notification of Significant Events
For the purposes of this clause, ‘Significant Event’ means:
any adverse comments or findings made by a court, commission, tribunal or other statutory or professional body regarding the conduct or performance of the Service Provider or its officers, employees, agents or Subcontractors that impacts or could be reasonably perceived to impact on their professional capacity, capability, fitness or reputation; or
any other significant matters, including the commencement of legal, regulatory or disciplinary action involving the Service Provider or its officers, employees, agents or Subcontractors, that may adversely impact on compliance with Commonwealth policy and legislation or the Commonwealth’s reputation.
The Service Provider must immediately issue the AEC a Notice on becoming aware of a Significant Event.
The Notice issued under clause 37C(b) must provide a summary of the Significant Event, including the date that it occurred and whether any Specified Personnel or other personnel engaged in connection with the Services were involved.   
The AEC may notify the Service Provider in writing that an event is to be considered a Significant Event for the purposes of this clause, and where this occurs the Service Provider must issue a Notice under clause 37C(b) in relation to the event within three (3) Business Days of being notified by the AEC.
Where reasonably requested by the AEC, the Service Provider must provide the AEC with any additional information regarding the Significant Event within three (3) Business Days of the request.
If requested by the AEC, the Service Provider must prepare a draft remediation plan and submit that draft plan to the AEC’s Representative for approval within ten (10) Business Days of the request.
A draft remediation plan prepared by the Service Provider under clause 37C(f) must include the following information:
how the Service Provider will address the Significant Event in the context of the Services, including confirmation that the implementation of the remediation plan will not in any way impact on the delivery of the Services or compliance by the Service Provider with its other obligations under this Deed; and
how the Service Provider will ensure events similar to the Significant Event do not occur again; and
any other matter reasonably requested by the AEC.
The AEC will review the draft remediation plan and either approve the draft remediation plan or provide the Service Provider with the details of any changes that are required. The Service Provider must make any changes to the draft remediation plan reasonably requested by the AEC and resubmit the draft remediation plan to the AEC for approval within three (3) Business Days of the request unless a different timeframe is agreed in writing by the AEC. This clause 37C(h) will apply to any resubmitted draft remediation plan.
Without limiting its other obligations under this Deed, the Service Provider must comply with the remediation plan as approved by the AEC. The Service Provider agrees to provide reports and other information about the Service Provider’s progress in implementing the remediation plan as reasonably requested by the AEC.
A failure by the Service Provider to comply with its obligations under this clause will be a material breach of this Deed. The AEC’s rights under this clause are in addition to and do not otherwise limit any other rights the AEC may have under this Deed. The performance by the Service Provider of its obligations under this clause will be at no additional cost to the AEC.

The Service Provider must:
effect and maintain the insurances specified in the Insurance Requirements for the period specified in the Insurance Requirements;
not reduce any insurance policy required by this Deed without the written consent of the AEC;
give evidence of the insurances, and their currency, acceptable to the AEC on request; and
ensure that any subcontract entered into by the Service Provider in relation to this Deed places on the subcontractor, in respect of the subcontractor's activities, the same or similar obligations about insurances, as this clause places on the Service Provider.
Force Majeure Events
Occurrence of Force Majeure Event
Subject to clause 39.2, a party (Affected Party) is excused from performing its obligations under this Deed to the extent it is prevented by circumstances beyond its reasonable control (other than lack of funds for any reason or any strike, lockout and labour disputes in respect of the Service Provider only), including acts of God, natural disasters, acts of war, riots and strikes outside the Affected Party's organisation (each a Force Majeure Event).
Notice of Force Majeure Event
When a Force Majeure Event arises or is reasonably perceived by the Affected Party as an imminent possibility, the Affected Party must give notice of the Force Majeure Event to the other party as soon as possible, identifying the effect it will have on its performance.
An Affected Party must make all reasonable efforts to minimise the effects of the Force Majeure Event on the performance of this Agreement.
Termination
If non-performance or diminished performance by the Affected Party due to a Force Majeure Event continues for a period of more than 30 consecutive days the other party may terminate the Agreement immediately by giving the Affected Party written notice.
Consequences of termination
If the Agreement is terminated under clause 39.3:
each party will bear its own costs and neither party will incur further liability to the other; and
where the Service Provider is the Affected Party, it will be entitled to payment for Services rendered in accordance with the Agreement prior to the date of intervention of the Force Majeure Event.
Dispute resolution
No arbitration or court proceedings
If a dispute arises in relation to the conduct of this Deed or an Agreement (Dispute), a party must comply with this clause 40 before starting arbitration or court proceedings (except proceedings for urgent interlocutory relief). After a party has sought or obtained any urgent interlocutory relief, that party must then follow this clause 40.
Notification
A party claiming a Dispute has arisen must give the other parties to the Dispute notice setting out details of the Dispute.
Parties to resolve Dispute
During the 14 days after a notice is given under clause 40.2 (or longer period if the parties to the Dispute agree in writing), each party to the Dispute must use its
reasonable efforts through a meeting of CEOs (or their nominees) to resolve the Dispute. If the parties cannot resolve the Dispute within that period, they must refer the Dispute to a mediator if one of them requests.
Appointment of mediator
If the parties to the Dispute cannot agree on a mediator within seven days after a request under clause 40.3, the chairperson of the Resolution Institute or the chairperson's nominee will appoint a mediator.
Role of mediator and obligations of parties
The role of a mediator is to assist in negotiating a resolution of the Dispute. A mediator may not make a binding decision on a party to the Dispute except if the party agrees in writing. Unless agreed by the mediator and parties, the mediation must be held within 21 days of the request for mediation in clause 40.3. The parties must attend the mediation and act in good faith to genuinely attempt to resolve the Dispute.
Confidentiality
Any information or documents disclosed by a party under this clause 40:
must be kept confidential; and
may only be used to attempt to resolve the Dispute.
Costs
Each party to a Dispute must pay its own costs of complying with this clause 40. The parties to the Dispute must equally pay the costs of any mediator.
Termination of process
A party to a Dispute may terminate the dispute resolution process by giving notice to each other party after it has complied with clauses 40.1 to 40.5. Clauses 40.6 and 40.7 survive termination of the dispute resolution process.
Breach of this clause
If a party to a Dispute breaches clauses 40.1 to 40.8, the other party does not have to comply with those clauses in relation to the Dispute.
Notices and other communications
Service of notices
A party giving notice or notifying under this Deed must do so in English and in writing:
as directed to the other party's contact person at the other party's address, as varied by any notice; and
hand delivered or sent by prepaid post, or Electronic Communication to that address.
The parties' addresses are specified in Item 12 of the Deed Details or as varied by a notice under this clause.
Effective on receipt
A notice given in accordance with clause 41.1 takes effect when it is taken to be received (or at a later time specified in the notice), and is taken to be received:
if hand delivered, on delivery;
if sent by prepaid post, on the second Business Day after the date of posting (or on the seventh Business Day after the date of posting if posted to or from a place outside Australia); or
if sent by Electronic Communication, at the time that would be the time of receipt under the Electronic Transactions Act 1999 (Cth),
but if the delivery, receipt or transmission is not urgent and not on a Business Day or is after 5.00pm on a Business Day, the notice is taken to be received at 9.00am on the next Business Day.

Schedule 1 - Deed Details
 Schedule 2 - Statement of Requirements	
Overview
Purpose
The role of the AEC is to deliver an Australian citizen's right to vote, as established and defined by the Commonwealth Electoral Act 1918 (CEA).
The AEC is responsible for conducting Electoral Events and maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll. The AEC also provides a range of electoral information and education programs and activities. These activities take place at various venues and locations across Australia.
During an Electoral Event, high volumes of furniture and equipment are hired and purchased by the AEC for use by each Division and State office. Only limited notice of an Electoral Event can be provided to the Service Provider.
In this context, the purpose of this Statement of Requirements (SoR) is to state the AEC’s requirements for Services in support of Electoral Events and invite potential suppliers to provide a solution that meets part or all of AEC’s requirements. 
About the AEC
The AEC has a three-tier office network structure comprising:
a National Office in Canberra;
6 state and two territory offices; and
currently 150 divisions located at approximately 85 locations around Australia.
	Additionally, for the conduct of an Electoral Event the AEC operates:
approximately 65 Out-Posted Centres predominantly in industrial buildings and warehouses; 
approximately 7,500 Polling Places at venues such as local schools and local halls, etc.
Further information about the AEC can be found on its website: http://www.aec.gov.au.
Definitions
In this SoR, the following additional definitions are used:
Services
Description of the Services
The AEC is seeking suitable Service Providers to provide the following Services:
Hire of furniture (e.g. tables, chairs) and/or office (e.g. water dispensers, urns, mats, lockers, etc.) and warehouse operational (e.g. pallet jacks, shelving, fencing, etc. – note, warehouse equipment exclusively at Out-Posted Centres) equipment (see Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT for further details); comprising:
delivery and collection of furniture and equipment;
unpacking/repacking and assembling furniture and equipment;
removal and disposal of packaging material;
meeting the AEC’s timeframes for Services;
dedicated account managers for critical stages within an Election Period; 
reporting on the Services; and
invoicing for the Services.
For noting: while ideally, the AEC would value Service Providers who can provide all the requirements noted in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT (i.e. all types of furniture and equipment, to all types of operation (OPCs and polling places) to all locations) the AEC understands that this may not reflect the business model of all Tenderers. 
As such, while it would value comprehensive offers (covering all operations, requirements and areas) the AEC still wishes to evaluate offers based on the industry specialisation and unique offerings reflected in the Tenderer’s submission. For example: furniture only offers, warehouse equipment only offers, purchase/l-buyback options, etc. 
The Pricing Schedules in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT reflect indicative usage totals for a future Event, while considering information from the most recent Event (Federal Election 2025), including:
a list of AEC locations and their demographic classifications
quantities
periods of hire.
Further indicative delivery graphics and profile information for statics can be found in Schedule 3 Annexure O of the RFT.
Delivery and collection of furniture and equipment to and from nominated venues and locations will generally be for fixed durations for all the required equipment. The delivery and duration requirement for some materials, however, will be staggered at OPCs (and possibly for other locations) depending on the needs for those locations (as an example, see ‘electoral furniture’ categories in the ‘Out-posted Centre’ tab of the spreadsheet template as part of Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT).
The AEC wishes to work with the successful Service Providers to develop efficient delivery patterns for furniture and equipment. This is also critical to assisting the AEC understand and plan for transport charges. Tenderers should factor time to work with AEC staff in each state to plan deliveries (e.g. milk runs) in the planning period directly prior to the election being called. 
Dedicated account manager(s) should have an appropriate level of seniority in the Service Provider’s organisation who is readily contactable and is experienced in performing a client relationship management role. They would be the primary point of contact for: 
managing the Services at a holistic level;
coordinating requests, Services and invoicing arrangements at the national level, where appropriate; and
addressing (and escalating where required) all correspondence, queries, complaints and concerns of the AEC Representative in a prompt and responsive manner.
The dedicated account manager must be available to:
liaise with the AEC Representative during Business Hours, after Business Hours, and on weekends during an Election Period; and
respond promptly to all AEC enquiries and requests.
An alternative contact person of equivalent seniority (or higher) must also be nominated by the Service Provider in the event that the dedicated account manager is not contactable.
On collection of furniture and equipment, the Service Provider must immediately notify the AEC if any AEC Material or Sensitive Election Material is found in the hired furniture or equipment. The AEC Material or Sensitive Election Material must be held securely by the Service Provider, until the AEC arranges for the collection of its material within 5 business days.
The Service Provider is expected to provide the Services in accordance with the AEC’s timeframes, specified in Table 1 below, noting the AEC will engage the Service Provider during planning stages in the lead up to a General Election to discuss this further. Final delivery timeframes will be confirmed prior to the requirement by the AEC in writing in a Work Order.
Table 1: AEC Delivery Timeframes for a General Election
Table 2 below provides an indicative schedule of the Services required from the Service Provider for the collection of hired furniture and equipment from Static Polling Places and PPVCs for a General Election.
Table 2: AEC Collection Timeframes for a General Election
The AEC may request the following Additional Services:
storage of furniture and equipment owned by the AEC; and
delivery, collection and/or transport of furniture and equipment and incidental goods and services.
The AEC may purchase Goods from the Service Provider at the prices set out in the relevant Schedule to the Agreement. A separate work order under the terms and conditions set out in the Commonwealth Contracting Suite will be established should the AEC choose to purchase any Goods. The terms and conditions for the Commonwealth Contracting Suite is at https://www.finance.gov.au/government/procurement/commonwealth-contracting-suite-ccs#ccs-terms. 
Additionally, the AEC wishes to consider options that involve the purchase of Goods from and sale-back to the Service Provider. The AEC asks Tenderers to use their industry expertise to develop options and provide pricing that allow the AEC to purchase the furniture and equipment and either retain that furniture and equipment, or, if there is no further ongoing requirement on the part of the AEC, for the Service Provider to arrange the pick-up and return of those items for a pre-agreed price. 
Should the Tenderer wish to tender for this element, the relevant pricing schedule should be added to Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT. Pricing should include the purchase price, delivery (transport and handling) pricing, sell-back pricing and pick up (transport and handling) pricing and any surcharges based on the range of furniture and equipment noted in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT.
Reporting
When delivering/collecting furniture and equipment, Service Providers must:
provide reports in a format, layout and style acceptable to the AEC. 
provide daily reports during peak service periods. The content of these reports will include an itemised list of completed deliveries/collections of furniture equipment to the AEC Representative on a daily basis (via email) before 6:00pm local time;
notify the AEC Representative immediately of any variation to the delivery or collection schedules whether due to circumstances within its control or otherwise;
ensure it has sufficient transport capacity and resources to ensure optimal trip and capacity planning to ensure the minimum number of trips are made, including working with the nominated AEC representative to agree delivery and pick up timeframes prior to execution;
ensure that the AEC Representative is contacted prior to the delivery or collection of the furniture and equipment where it is anticipated that additional charges (including any surcharges, as noted in the pricing schedule) may apply, for example as a result of additional trips or weight.
Invoicing
Invoices must meet the requirements of a tax invoice as set out in the GST Act and contain the following information:
the details of the Services e.g. quantities/descriptions/locations/dates/times of the furniture delivered;
contract number;
period covered by invoice;
title and description of the Services;
the amount of any Fee Credits applicable during the invoice period; 
surcharges (including details of those surcharges e.g. justification, location, dates, times) and
any other information reasonably requested by the AEC from time to time.
Service Levels
The AEC requires the Service Provider to meet or exceed agreed Service Levels as detailed in Table 3 with final measurement levels for Service Levels to be included in the proposed agreement.
Table 3 – Draft Service Levels
Note to Tenderers: The AEC will work with the Service Provider to develop a Service Level Agreement (SLA) based on the service levels above. The SLA will involve the application of fee credits should the Service Provider fail to meet the service levels. It is expected that the Service Provider will monitor and report on its performance with the intent to meet or exceed all service levels and actively identify any areas requiring improvement.
For further guidance on the AEC’s current thoughts on developing the Service Level Agreement, please see the Annexure A – Service Level Scenario.
 Annexure A -  Service Level Scenario	
Background
The key constraint to achieving optimal outcomes for furniture and equipment hire concerns uncertainty around the timing of an Electoral Event i.e. the AEC cannot give clear notice of when the next General Election will be held. At this stage, the best information that can be provided to Service Providers on timing is that:
the earliest the next General Election can be called is 7 August 2027; and
the latest time that the next General Election can be called is 20 May 2028.
AEC will also provide guidance on subsequent Electoral Events including General Elections when known.
The Services will primarily be provided in support of three categories of venues with different levels of uncertainty surrounding their requirements:
Out-Posted Centres (OPCs): typically, premises for an OPC are leased for a longer period (e.g. 1 to 3 months) than PPVCs or Static Polling Places. As ballot papers are counted at an OPC they must be secure. There is higher certainty around the furniture/equipment requirements for an OPC.
 Pre-Poll Voting Centre (PPVC): a polling place where an elector casts a vote before Polling Day. Typically leases for PPVCs are approximately 1 month. Premises leased are mostly community centres, schools and churches. Certainty around the furniture/equipment requirements for a PPVC is low but considered better than that for Static Polling Places noting that some PPVC locations will not be finalised until after the General Election announcement has been made.
Static Polling Places: polling places set up in each Division to take the votes of electors for an Election Event i.e. the venue where voters will turn up on Polling Day. Premises for Static Polling Places are often local schools and churches. Leases for Static Polling Places are very short meaning timeframes for delivery and collection of furniture will be limited (e.g. 1-2 days). Static Polling Places have the lowest level of certainty around their furniture/equipment requirements.
Managing uncertainty
It is estimated that the AEC's requirements for approximately 80% of its furniture and equipment needs will be related to OPCs. Noting this, the AEC proposes to manage this uncertainty related to its OPC requirements by:
Engaging closely with Service Providers during planning stages. For example once leases for OPCs are finalised, the Service Provider will be provided the relevant details to help with the delivery/collection schedule; and
Work Orders for OPCs should be established relatively early in the lead up to a General Election i.e. several months prior to a General Election.
The AEC's furniture and equipment requirements for Pre-Poll Voting Centres and Static Polling Places are far more 'fluid' and cannot be fixed until after the announcement has been made. Noting that timeliness for delivery of furniture and equipment is critical, the AEC proposes to manage this uncertainty by:
Providing Service Providers maximum/minimum ranges for its furniture and equipment requirements;
Establishing Work Orders on a Not-To-Exceed basis and drawing down on their value as requirements become fixed;
Selecting Service Providers that will proactively engage with the AEC to actively manage uncertainty and risk.
Service level agreement
As Polling Day draws closer, Service Providers may be unable to meet the AEC's requirements if they have been given insufficient time and the AEC's options to approach alternative suppliers will be restricted.
To reduce the likelihood of this scenario and to better align the interests of the AEC and the Service Provider, a Service Level Agreement is at Schedule 4 of this Deed.
For the purposes of developing an effective Service Level Agreement an example of an optimal outcome for Static Polling Places in a Division is provided below.
Example of Optimal Outcome for Static Polling Places in a Division
An Election Event is announced and the Service Provider is notified. Within 7 days, most of the orders confirming the AEC's requirements are confirmed for each location within the Division. However, some orders can only be provided closer to Polling Day.
Service Providers coordinate the necessary furniture and equipment in line with AEC requirements.
Furniture and equipment is delivered to Static Polling Places with all deliveries being finalised by 8pm on the Friday before Polling Day (Saturday).
At delivery, the Service Provider unpacks the furniture, assembles the furniture, moves it to the approximate vicinity of the desired place within the Static Polling Place and then removes all packaging material.
Furniture and equipment is marked/labelled as belonging to the Service Provider.
The Service Provider obtains approval/signature from the nominated AEC representative at the site that the furniture/equipment has been delivered.
By 6pm on each day during the 2 weeks leading up to Polling Day, the Service Provider emails the relevant AEC representative of all furniture and equipment that has been delivered (Delivery Report). However, on the Friday before Polling Day, 2 Delivery Reports are provided: one at 1pm and one at 9pm.
The day following Polling Day (Sunday), collection of equipment commences at venues where this is possible - some venues may be unable to facilitate collection on Sundays. Photos are taken of any damaged furniture or equipment.
All furniture/equipment has been collected by the second day following Polling Day (Monday).
By 6pm on each collection day, the Service Provider emails the relevant AEC representative of all furniture and equipment that has been collected which includes details of any missing furniture/equipment as well as photos of damaged equipment (Collection Report).
In the following weeks, an invoice is then sent by the Service Provider to the AEC detailing all furniture and equipment that has been delivered and collected from the relevant sites. The details in this invoice can be clearly
reconciled to the Delivery/Collection reports as well as the AEC's initial orders. No surcharges or levies are included that have not been agreed with the AEC prior and such surcharges and levies, if included, can be clearly and easily traced back to determine how they were incurred.
Some potential factors leading to sub-optimal outcomes in this example are provided below:
The Service Provider is unable to meet the AEC's requirements for furniture and equipment in terms of quality and quantity leading to the AEC approaching other suppliers;
Faulty furniture or equipment is reported to the Service Provider but action taken to replace the faulty equipment is delayed;
Furniture and equipment is not marked accurately which leads to confusion during collection and preparing Collection Reports;
Invoices provide insufficient detail and do not reconcile with Collection Reports or Delivery Reports, including surcharges and levies.

include all costs and expenses necessary and incidental to the proper delivery of the Services.
 Schedule 4 - Service Level Agreement	
Overview
Definitions
In this Service Level Agreement, the following additional definitions apply:
Fee Credit Matrix	the matrix at Item 5 of this Service Level Agreement.
Fee Credit Pool	the maximum amount of Fee Credits that may be claimed by the AEC, calculated as 10 per cent of the total Fees payable for each invoice.
Fee Credit mechanism
In providing the Services, the Service Provider must meet or exceed the Service Levels.
For the avoidance of doubt, the Service Levels apply equally to the Services, and any other Additional Services (as relevant) procured under the Agreement from time to time.
The AEC requires the Service Provider to measure and report on Service Levels as a means of indicating performance and identifying any areas requiring improvement.
The AEC requires that Fee Credits are applied to Service Levels in accordance with the Fee Credit Matrix.
Service Provider responsibilities
The Service Provider's performance of the Services will be monitored by the AEC and, where possible, as identified by the AEC or the Service Provider, improved over the Agreement Period.
It is expected that the Service Provider will monitor and report on its performance with the intent to meet or exceed all Service Levels and actively identifying any areas requiring improvement.
If the Service Provider fails to accurately perform the monitoring, recording and reporting tasks that are necessary for the purpose of calculating the achieved Service Levels and Fee Credits, those Services will be regarded as not having been delivered.
The Service Provider will be required as part of its responsibilities under this Service Level Agreement to:
implement effective tools to measure performance for each Service Level;
monitor and report performance against each Service Level;
allocate the required resources to monitor and measure performance;
demonstrate mitigating circumstances where performance is impacted by events outside the Service Provider's control. Evidence of mitigating circumstances does not automatically provide relief from any consequence resulting from a failure to meet a Service Level. However, evidence of such events will be carefully considered by the AEC during performance reviews; and
submit monthly Service Level Reports to the AEC Contract Manager confirming:
to what extent each of the Service Levels have been met during the previous month and the number and frequency of any failures to meet the Service Levels with a detailed explanation of the failure(s);
what measures the Service Provider has put in place where Service Levels have not been met to ensure that Service Levels are met (or exceeded) in the future; and
the Fee Credits to be applied for the previous month.
All Service Levels must be measured in accordance with the requirements stated for each Service Level in Item 4 of this Service Level Agreement.
The Service Provider is responsible for end to end Incident management of escalated Incidents to its subcontractors and any impact on the Service Levels will be the responsibility of the Service Provider.
Source data
The Service Levels provide an indication of the source data that may be used to calculate and determine the extent to which a Service Level has been met by the Service Provider.
Notwithstanding any item listed as source data, the AEC at all times reserves its right to rely on any creditable data source (including its own records) to determine the extent to which a Service Level has been met by the Service Provider.
Fee Credits
Introduction
The AEC is entitled to Fee Credits where the Service Provider fails to achieve the Service Levels.
The Fee Credits that apply for failure to meet the Service Levels must be applied to the monthly invoice amount (excluding approved expenses).
In addition to compensating the AEC for a lower than agreed level of Service, Fee Credits also act as an incentive for the Service Provider to meet the Service Levels. As such, the Service Levels have two trigger points:
the first trigger point signifies a degraded level of Service, for which a specified part of the Fee Credit may be applied at the AEC's discretion; and
the second trigger point is the "failure point". At this trigger point the entire Service Level has failed and the full Fee Credit (or the residual amount if the AEC already applied a part of the Fee Credit due to degraded Service) may be applied at the AEC's discretion.
Application of Fee Credits
The AEC's primary aim is to use the Fee Credit mechanism as a means to continuously improve the effectiveness of Services provided by the Service Provider. In the application of Fee Credits, emphasis will be placed on bringing performance up to agreed and acceptable levels rather than on achieving a reduction in Fees.
Where the Service Provider fails any Service Level the Service Provider will be required to provide a Service Action Plan (within 5 Business Days of notifying the AEC of the Service Level failure) outlining the steps it is taking or will take to rectify the situation.
Notwithstanding the provision of any acceptable Service Action Plan by the Service Provider, all Fee Credits applicable to those Service Levels may be claimed by the AEC.
Prior to claiming applicable Fee Credits, the AEC will be required to consider any mitigating circumstances relating to the Service Provider's failure to meet the agreed Service Level(s). The quantum of Fee Credits payable may be reduced, at the AEC's discretion, based on the information provided by the Service Provider in relation to the mitigating circumstances. Failure to provide, or inability to secure, adequate resources to meet the Service Level will not be considered a mitigating circumstance.
Fee Credit mechanism
The Fee Credit Pool is allocated across the Service Levels such that the distribution totals 100% of the Fee Credit Pool.
A failure to meet a Service Level's "first trigger" will generate a Fee Credit for that Service Level in accordance with the Fee Credit Matrix.
A failure to meet a Service Level's "second trigger" will generate a Fee Credit equal to the residual amount of the Fee Credit for that Service Level in accordance with the Fee Credit Matrix.
The AEC may claim the full Fee Credit for a Service Level due to failure to meet both the "first trigger" and the "second trigger" without having to issue a separate claim regarding the failure of each "trigger".
A Fee Credit will be discharged by:
crediting the amount on the subsequent monthly invoice (or the next applicable invoice); or
in the event there are no further invoices for that Electoral Event or the invoice amount is less than the applicable Fee Credits, making payment of the applicable amount to the AEC.
Changes to Fee Credit Pool distribution
The AEC reserves the right to adjust the Fee Credit Pool distribution across the Service Levels every three months at the AEC's discretion. The reasons for adjusting the Fee Credit Pool distribution will be limited to:
addition or removal of Service Levels in accordance with Item 3.3 of this Service Level Agreement;
reprioritisation of categories and/or individual Service Level percentages to target the Service Provider's service delivery performance to encourage improved performance; and
mitigating changes in assessed risks in the delivery the Services by the Service Provider.
Any redistribution will not alter the size of the Fee Credit Pool. The AEC will advise the Service Provider in writing, one month before the new distribution will take effect.
Overview of Service Levels

Summary of Service Levels

Changes to Service Levels
The AEC may request additional Service Levels, changes or deletions of Service Levels at a minimum, every three months by advising the Service Provider in writing of the changes proposed by the AEC.
Where the AEC requests a new Service Level, the Service Provider will meet with the AEC and agreement will be reached to implement this change.
Where a new Service Level is similar to other Service Levels already in place, the AEC expects that the minimum requirements for the new Service Level will be no lower than the similar Service Levels, unless agreed to by the AEC.

Worked examples
Example 1:
The Fee Credit Pool is $1000 and Service Level 1 is Triggered. The Fee Credit the AEC is entitled to is: ($1000 x 40% of the Fee Credit allocation for Service Level1) x 30% (for the first trigger point) = $120
Example 2:
The Fee Credit Pool is $15,000 and Service Level 1 is triggered on the second occasion. The Fee Credit the AEC is entitled to is: ($15,000 x 40% of the Fee Credit Pool a/location for Service Level 1) x 70% (given both the first and second triggers have occurred and the first trigger has already been claimed) = $4,200.

Schedule 1 - Statement of Requirements	
Overview
Purpose
The role of the AEC is to deliver an Australian citizen's right to vote, as established and defined by the Commonwealth Electoral Act 1918 (CEA).
The AEC is responsible for conducting Electoral Events and maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll. The AEC also provides a range of electoral information and education programs and activities. These activities take place at various venues and locations across Australia.
During an Electoral Event, high volumes of furniture and equipment are hired and purchased by the AEC for use by each Division and State office. Only limited notice of an Electoral Event can be provided to the Service Provider.
In this context, the purpose of this Statement of Requirements (SoR) is to state the AEC’s requirements for Services in support of Electoral Events and invite potential suppliers to provide a solution that meets part or all of AEC’s requirements. 
About the AEC
The AEC has a three-tier office network structure comprising:
a National Office in Canberra;
6 state and two territory offices; and
currently 150 divisions located at approximately 85 locations around Australia.
	Additionally, for the conduct of an Electoral Event the AEC operates:
approximately 65 Out-Posted Centres predominantly in industrial buildings and warehouses; 
approximately 7,500 Polling Places at venues such as local schools and local halls, etc.
Further information about the AEC can be found on its website: http://www.aec.gov.au.
Definitions
In this SoR, the following additional definitions are used:
Services
Description of the Services
The AEC is seeking suitable Service Providers to provide the following Services:
Hire of furniture (e.g. tables, chairs) and/or office (e.g. water dispensers, urns, mats, lockers, etc.) and warehouse operational (e.g. pallet jacks, shelving, fencing, etc. – note, warehouse equipment exclusively at Out-Posted Centres) equipment (see Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT for further details); comprising:
delivery and collection of furniture and equipment;
unpacking/repacking and assembling furniture and equipment;
removal and disposal of packaging material;
meeting the AEC’s timeframes for Services;
dedicated account managers for critical stages within an Election Period; 
reporting on the Services; and
invoicing for the Services.
For noting: while ideally, the AEC would value Service Providers who can provide all the requirements noted in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT (i.e. all types of furniture and equipment, to all types of operation (OPCs and polling places) to all locations) the AEC understands that this may not reflect the business model of all Tenderers. 
As such, while it would value comprehensive offers (covering all operations, requirements and areas) the AEC still wishes to evaluate offers based on the industry specialisation and unique offerings reflected in the Tenderer’s submission. For example: furniture only offers, warehouse equipment only offers, purchase/l-buyback options, etc. 
The Pricing Schedules in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT reflect indicative usage totals for a future Event, while considering information from the most recent Event (Federal Election 2025), including:
a list of AEC locations and their demographic classifications
quantities
periods of hire.
Further indicative delivery graphics and profile information for statics can be found in Schedule 3 Annexure O of the RFT.
Delivery and collection of furniture and equipment to and from nominated venues and locations will generally be for fixed durations for all the required equipment. The delivery and duration requirement for some materials, however, will be staggered at OPCs (and possibly for other locations) depending on the needs for those locations (as an example, see ‘electoral furniture’ categories in the ‘Out-posted Centre’ tab of the spreadsheet template as part of Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT).
The AEC wishes to work with the successful Service Providers to develop efficient delivery patterns for furniture and equipment. This is also critical to assisting the AEC understand and plan for transport charges. Tenderers should factor time to work with AEC staff in each state to plan deliveries (e.g. milk runs) in the planning period directly prior to the election being called. 
Dedicated account manager(s) should have an appropriate level of seniority in the Service Provider’s organisation who is readily contactable and is experienced in performing a client relationship management role. They would be the primary point of contact for: 
managing the Services at a holistic level;
coordinating requests, Services and invoicing arrangements at the national level, where appropriate; and
addressing (and escalating where required) all correspondence, queries, complaints and concerns of the AEC Representative in a prompt and responsive manner.
The dedicated account manager must be available to:
liaise with the AEC Representative during Business Hours, after Business Hours, and on weekends during an Election Period; and
respond promptly to all AEC enquiries and requests.
An alternative contact person of equivalent seniority (or higher) must also be nominated by the Service Provider in the event that the dedicated account manager is not contactable.
On collection of furniture and equipment, the Service Provider must immediately notify the AEC if any AEC Material or Sensitive Election Material is found in the hired furniture or equipment. The AEC Material or Sensitive Election Material must be held securely by the Service Provider, until the AEC arranges for the collection of its material within 5 business days.
The Service Provider is expected to provide the Services in accordance with the AEC’s timeframes, specified in Table 1 below, noting the AEC will engage the Service Provider during planning stages in the lead up to a General Election to discuss this further. Final delivery timeframes will be confirmed prior to the requirement by the AEC in writing in a Work Order.
Table 1: AEC Delivery Timeframes for a General Election
Table 2 below provides an indicative schedule of the Services required from the Service Provider for the collection of hired furniture and equipment from Static Polling Places and PPVCs for a General Election.
Table 2: AEC Collection Timeframes for a General Election
The AEC may request the following Additional Services:
storage of furniture and equipment owned by the AEC; and
delivery, collection and/or transport of furniture and equipment and incidental goods and services.
The AEC may purchase Goods from the Service Provider at the prices set out in the relevant Schedule to the Agreement. A separate work order under the terms and conditions set out in the Commonwealth Contracting Suite will be established should the AEC choose to purchase any Goods. The terms and conditions for the Commonwealth Contracting Suite is at https://www.finance.gov.au/government/procurement/commonwealth-contracting-suite-ccs#ccs-terms. 
Additionally, the AEC wishes to consider options that involve the purchase of Goods from and sale-back to the Service Provider. The AEC asks Tenderers to use their industry expertise to develop options and provide pricing that allow the AEC to purchase the furniture and equipment and either retain that furniture and equipment, or, if there is no further ongoing requirement on the part of the AEC, for the Service Provider to arrange the pick-up and return of those items for a pre-agreed price. 
Should the Tenderer wish to tender for this element, the relevant pricing schedule should be added to Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT. Pricing should include the purchase price, delivery (transport and handling) pricing, sell-back pricing and pick up (transport and handling) pricing and any surcharges based on the range of furniture and equipment noted in Schedule 3 Annexure D of the RFT.
Reporting
When delivering/collecting furniture and equipment, Service Providers must:
provide reports in a format, layout and style acceptable to the AEC. 
provide daily reports during peak service periods. The content of these reports will include an itemised list of completed deliveries/collections of furniture equipment to the AEC Representative on a daily basis (via email) before 6:00pm local time;
notify the AEC Representative immediately of any variation to the delivery or collection schedules whether due to circumstances within its control or otherwise;
ensure it has sufficient transport capacity and resources to ensure optimal trip and capacity planning to ensure the minimum number of trips are made, including working with the nominated AEC representative to agree delivery and pick up timeframes prior to execution;
ensure that the AEC Representative is contacted prior to the delivery or collection of the furniture and equipment where it is anticipated that additional charges (including any surcharges, as noted in the pricing schedule) may apply, for example as a result of additional trips or weight.
Invoicing
Invoices must meet the requirements of a tax invoice as set out in the GST Act and contain the following information:
the details of the Services e.g. quantities/descriptions/locations/dates/times of the furniture delivered;
contract number;
period covered by invoice;
title and description of the Services;
the amount of any Fee Credits applicable during the invoice period; 
surcharges (including details of those surcharges e.g. justification, location, dates, times) and
any other information reasonably requested by the AEC from time to time.
Service Levels
The AEC requires the Service Provider to meet or exceed agreed Service Levels as detailed in Table 3 with final measurement levels for Service Levels to be included in the proposed agreement.
Table 3 – Draft Service Levels

The SLA will involve the application of fee credits should the Service Provider fail to meet the service levels. It is expected that the Service Provider will monitor and report on its performance with the intent to meet or exceed all service levels and actively identify any areas requiring improvement.
For further guidance on the AEC’s current thoughts on developing the Service Level Agreement, please see the Annexure A – Service Level Scenario.
 Annexure A -  Service Level Scenario	
Background
The key constraint to achieving optimal outcomes for furniture and equipment hire concerns uncertainty around the timing of an Electoral Event i.e. the AEC cannot give clear notice of when the next General Election will be held. At this stage, the best information that can be provided to Service Providers on timing is that:
the earliest the next General Election can be called is 7 August 2027; and
the latest time that the next General Election can be called is 20 May 2028.
AEC will also provide guidance on subsequent Electoral Events including General Elections when known.
The Services will primarily be provided in support of three categories of venues with different levels of uncertainty surrounding their requirements:
Out-Posted Centres (OPCs): typically, premises for an OPC are leased for a longer period (e.g. 1 to 3 months) than PPVCs or Static Polling Places. As ballot papers are counted at an OPC they must be secure. There is higher certainty around the furniture/equipment requirements for an OPC.
 Pre-Poll Voting Centre (PPVC): a polling place where an elector casts a vote before Polling Day. Typically leases for PPVCs are approximately 1 month. Premises leased are mostly community centres, schools and churches. Certainty around the furniture/equipment requirements for a PPVC is low but considered better than that for Static Polling Places noting that some PPVC locations will not be finalised until after the General Election announcement has been made.
Static Polling Places: polling places set up in each Division to take the votes of electors for an Election Event i.e. the venue where voters will turn up on Polling Day. Premises for Static Polling Places are often local schools and churches. Leases for Static Polling Places are very short meaning timeframes for delivery and collection of furniture will be limited (e.g. 1-2 days). Static Polling Places have the lowest level of certainty around their furniture/equipment requirements.
Managing uncertainty
It is estimated that the AEC's requirements for approximately 80% of its furniture and equipment needs will be related to OPCs. Noting this, the AEC proposes to manage this uncertainty related to its OPC requirements by:
Engaging closely with Service Providers during planning stages. For example once leases for OPCs are finalised, the Service Provider will be provided the relevant details to help with the delivery/collection schedule; and
Work Orders for OPCs should be established relatively early in the lead up to a General Election i.e. several months prior to a General Election.
The AEC's furniture and equipment requirements for Pre-Poll Voting Centres and Static Polling Places are far more 'fluid' and cannot be fixed until after the announcement has been made. Noting that timeliness for delivery of furniture and equipment is critical, the AEC proposes to manage this uncertainty by:
Providing Service Providers maximum/minimum ranges for its furniture and equipment requirements;
Establishing Work Orders on a Not-To-Exceed basis and drawing down on their value as requirements become fixed;
Selecting Service Providers that will proactively engage with the AEC to actively manage uncertainty and risk.
Service level agreement
As Polling Day draws closer, Service Providers may be unable to meet the AEC's requirements if they have been given insufficient time and the AEC's options to approach alternative suppliers will be restricted.
To reduce the likelihood of this scenario and to better align the interests of the AEC and the Service Provider, a Service Level Agreement is at Schedule 4 of this Deed.
For the purposes of developing an effective Service Level Agreement an example of an optimal outcome for Static Polling Places in a Division is provided below.
Example of Optimal Outcome for Static Polling Places in a Division
An Election Event is announced and the Service Provider is notified. Within 7 days, most of the orders confirming the AEC's requirements are confirmed for each location within the Division. However, some orders can only be provided closer to Polling Day.
Service Providers coordinate the necessary furniture and equipment in line with AEC requirements.
Furniture and equipment is delivered to Static Polling Places with all deliveries being finalised by 8pm on the Friday before Polling Day (Saturday).
At delivery, the Service Provider unpacks the furniture, assembles the furniture, moves it to the approximate vicinity of the desired place within the Static Polling Place and then removes all packaging material.
Furniture and equipment is marked/labelled as belonging to the Service Provider.
The Service Provider obtains approval/signature from the nominated AEC representative at the site that the furniture/equipment has been delivered.
By 6pm on each day during the 2 weeks leading up to Polling Day, the Service Provider emails the relevant AEC representative of all furniture and equipment that has been delivered (Delivery Report). However, on the Friday before Polling Day, 2 Delivery Reports are provided: one at 1pm and one at 9pm.
The day following Polling Day (Sunday), collection of equipment commences at venues where this is possible - some venues may be unable to facilitate collection on Sundays. Photos are taken of any damaged furniture or equipment.
All furniture/equipment has been collected by the second day following Polling Day (Monday).
By 6pm on each collection day, the Service Provider emails the relevant AEC representative of all furniture and equipment that has been collected which includes details of any missing furniture/equipment as well as photos of damaged equipment (Collection Report).
In the following weeks, an invoice is then sent by the Service Provider to the AEC detailing all furniture and equipment that has been delivered and collected from the relevant sites. The details in this invoice can be clearly
Some potential factors leading to sub-optimal outcomes in this example are provided below:
The Service Provider is unable to meet the AEC's requirements for furniture and equipment in terms of quality and quantity leading to the AEC approaching other suppliers;
Faulty furniture or equipment is reported to the Service Provider but action taken to replace the faulty equipment is delayed;
Furniture and equipment is not marked accurately which leads to confusion during collection and preparing Collection Reports;
Invoices provide insufficient detail and do not reconcile with Collection Reports or Delivery Reports, including surcharges and levies.

Request for Tender – Terms and Conditions	
RFT details
Overview
Introduction
The Commonwealth of Australia represented by the Australian Electoral Commission (AEC) invites Tenders to respond to this Request for Tender (RFT) for the Services.
Detailed information on the AEC's requirements for the Services is contained at Schedule 1 to this RFT (Statement of Requirements).  
Tenderers should refer to clause 2.3 when reading this RFT for guidance on defined terms and how clauses are to be interpreted. 
Tenders should be in the form set out in Schedule 3, including all of its Annexures.
Tenders should be submitted in accordance with clause 5 of this RFT. The AEC will exclude from the evaluation process a Tender that does not satisfy each of the Mandatory Requirements.  
Tenders will be assessed against the Evaluation Criteria.
About the AEC
The AEC is a Commonwealth agency responsible for conducting federal Electoral Events and maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll. The AEC is also responsible for undertaking electoral research, promoting community awareness of electoral and Parliamentary matters, and assisting in the conduct of approved international elections and industrial election events.
The AEC's website is at http://www.aec.gov.au.  AEC offices are organised geographically, with a National Office in Canberra, seven state and territory offices and Divisional Offices in or near each of the electoral Divisions.
Definitions and interpretation
Unless the context dictates otherwise:
terms defined in the Deed of Standing Offer or the Statement of Requirements have the same meaning in this RFT; and
the interpretation clause of the Deed of Standing Offer applies to the interpretation of this RFT.
All other capitalised terms are defined as follows for the purposes of this RFT. To the extent of any inconsistency, terms defined below will prevail over those in the Deed of Standing Offer or Statement of Requirements.
If there is any inconsistency between any provisions of this RFT the following provisions will prevail in descending order, to the extent of the inconsistency:
these Request for Tender – Terms and Conditions (clauses 1 to 10);
Schedule 2 – Draft Deed of Standing Offer;
Schedule 1 – Statement of Requirements; and
Schedule 3 – Tender Form.
Objectives and expected outcomes
The AEC expects to identify Service Providers to deliver the Services on a best value for money overall basis. 
The AEC intends to set up a panel of Service Providers, all of whom are engaged under a Deed of Standing Offer. Further information on panel arrangements is available on the Department of Finance website.
The proposed Initial Deed Period of the Deed of Standing Offer is set out in Item 7, with the Deed Option Period specified in Item 7 (if any), exercisable at the AEC’s discretion. 
Partial Tenders will only be accepted if specified in Item 8.
Indicative timetable
The proposed timetable for the release of this RFT and submission of Tenders is set out in Items 2 and 3. 
Details of any industry briefing are set out in Item 11.
The proposed timetable for the Tender evaluation process is set out in Item 9. This proposed timetable is provided for convenience only and does not identify all activities relating to this RFT process or the evaluation of Tenders. The AEC may change the proposed timetable at its absolute discretion without notification to Tenderers.

The notification under clause 2.6(a) is for the AEC’s information purposes only.
Failure to provide the notification under clause 2.6(a) will not preclude a Tenderer from submitting a Tender in response to this RFT.

Contracting with a single legal entity 
The AEC intends that any Deed of Standing Offer that is awarded will be between the AEC and a single legal entity (as opposed to a group of entities or a consortium), with that single legal entity being responsible for the performance of all of the Service Provider’s obligations contained in the Deed of Standing Offer.
Where the Tenderer proposes to leverage off multiple entities to provide the Services, those entities must be subcontractors to the single legal entity who contracts with the AEC.
The AEC may exclude a Tender from the evaluation process if the Tenderer does not propose to contract with the AEC using a single legal entity that exists at the RFT Closing Time. 
Alternative Tenders
This clause 4.7 applies where Item 9 indicates that alternative Tenders will be accepted for the purposes of this RFT.
A Tenderer must submit a Tender that complies with the requirements of this RFT. However the Tenderer may also submit an alternative Tender which does not comply with the requirements of this RFT provided that a compliant Tender is also submitted. 
If a Tenderer submits an alternative Tender, they should clearly identify it as an alternative Tender and clearly state the advantages, disadvantages, limitations and capabilities of the alternative Tender.

RFT Closing Time

Tenders lodged by any other means (including physically or by email) will not be accepted.
Preparing to lodge a Tender 
	Tender file formats, naming conventions and sizes
The AEC will accept Tenders lodged in Microsoft Word, Microsoft Excel and/or PDF format.
The Tender file name(s):
should incorporate the Tenderer’s legal entity name; and
should reflect the various parts of the bid they represent, where the Tender comprises multiple files.        
Tender files must not exceed a combined file size of seven megabytes per upload. 
Tenders must be completely self-contained. No hyperlinked or other material may be incorporated by reference. 
Checking for Harmful Code
In submitting their Tenders electronically, Tenderers warrant that they have taken reasonable steps to ensure that Tender files are free of Harmful Code which may affect AusTender and/or the AEC’s ICT environment. Tenders found to contain Harmful Code may be excluded from consideration.
Electronic lodgement process
Tenderers must allow sufficient time for Tender lodgement, including time that may be required for any problem analysis and resolution prior to the RFT Closing Time.
Tenderers should be aware that holding the “Lodge a Response” page in the web browser will not hold the electronic tender box open beyond the RFT Closing Time. An error message will be issued if the lodgement process is attempted after the RFT Closing Time.
Tenders lodged through AusTender will be deemed to be authorised by the Tenderer.
If Tenderers have any problem in accessing the AusTender website or uploading a Tender they should contact the AusTender Help Desk prior to the RFT Closing Time. Failure to do so may result in the Tender being excluded from consideration.
Late Tenders, incomplete Tenders and corrupted files
Any attempt to lodge a Tender after the RFT Closing Time will not be permitted by AusTender. Such a Tender will be deemed a Late Tender. Late Tenders will be excluded from consideration unless the Tender is a Late Tender as a consequence of mishandling by the AEC.
The countdown clock displayed on AusTender will display in real time the amount of time left until the RFT Closing Time. The time displayed on the countdown clock is deemed the correct amount of time left until the RFT Closing Time and will be the means by which the AEC will determine whether a Tender has been lodged by the RFT Closing Time.
Where electronic submission of a Tender has commenced prior to the RFT Closing Time but concluded after the RFT Closing Time, and upload of the Tender file/s has completed successfully, as confirmed by AusTender system logs, the Tender will not be deemed to be a Late Tender. Such Tenders will be identified by AusTender to the AEC as having commenced transmission prior to, but completed lodgement after, the RFT Closing Time.
Where a Tender lodgement consists of multiple uploads, due to the number and/or size of the files, Tenderers must ensure that transmission of all files is completed and receipted before the RFT Closing Time and clause 5.5(c) will only apply to the final upload.
Incomplete Tenders may be excluded from consideration. This includes Tenders with electronic files that cannot be read or decrypted.
If the AEC believes a Tender to potentially contain any Harmful Code or anything else that might compromise the integrity or security of AusTender and/or the AEC’s ICT environment, it may also be excluded from consideration.
Proof of electronic lodgement
When a Tender lodgement has successfully completed, an official receipt is provided on-screen. The on-screen receipt will record the time and date the Tender was received by AusTender and will be conclusive evidence of successful lodgement of a Tender. It is essential that Tenderers save and print this receipt as proof of lodgement. A separate email confirming receipt of the Tender will also be automatically dispatched to the email address of the registered user whose details were recorded at login.
Failure to receive a receipt means that lodgement has not completed successfully. Where no receipt has been issued by AusTender, the attempted lodgement will be deemed to have been unsuccessful. Tenderers should refer to clauses 5.4(a) and 5.4(d). 
AusTender security

Tenderers acknowledge that:
lodgement of their Tender on time and in accordance with this RFT is entirely their responsibility; and
the AEC will not be liable for any loss, damage, costs or expenses incurred by Tenderers or any other person if, for any reason, a Tender or any other material or communication relevant to this RFT is not received on time, is corrupted or altered or otherwise is not received as sent, cannot be read or decrypted, or has its security or integrity compromised.
It is the responsibility of the Tenderers to ensure that their infrastructure including operating system and browser revision levels meet the minimum standards as defined on AusTender. Neither the AEC nor the Commonwealth take any responsibility for any problems arising from Tenderers’ infrastructure and/or internet connectivity.
Corrections and additions
Tenders containing pricing or other information that is not stated clearly and legibly may be excluded from consideration.

Clarification of Tenders by the AEC

respond to any request for clarification within the time period and in the format specified by the AEC;
ensure that clarifying information provided answers the AEC's enquiry and is consistent with the Tender submitted by the Tenderer; and
